ῬΑΒΑΜΒΌΠΕ ῬΒΕΑΜΒΌΤΕ ნხხტMნსსს ΡΆΒΑΜΒΌΠΕ ῬΡῬΒΕΑΜΒΌΙΕ Τοὺς περὶ εὐλογιῶν τοῦ ᾿Ιακὼδ ποιούμενος λόγους αὐτός, αὐτὸς τὴν παρὰ τοῦ κυρίου εὐλογίαν αἰτῶ. τίς γὰρ ἀνθρώπων ἱκανὸς διηγήσασθαι τὰ πνευματικῶς εἰρη- μένα, εἰ μὴ ἣ οὐράνιος σοφία συνεργεῖ τῷ λέγοντι; τίς δὲ ἑρμηνεῦσαι δύναται τὰ διὰ τῶν μακαρίων προφητῶν διὰ παραδολῶν αἰνιγματωδῶς εἰρημένα, εἰ μὴ αὐτὸς ὁ λόγος ἑαυτοῦ ἑρμηνεὺς γένηται; ὃς πάλαι μὲν « ἐν τῷ >> Ἰσραὴλ ὡς λύχνος ἐν τῷ νόμῳ ὑπὸ τὸν μόδιον κρυπτόμενος ἵνα τοὺς ἐν νόμῳ διὰ τῆς περιτομῆς δικαιουμένους φω- τίσῃ, νυνὶ δὲ παρρησίᾳ ἐπὶ τοῦ ξύλου L ὡς ἐν λυχνίᾳ ἑπτάμυξος λύχνος φαινόμε- νος, L ἐξ ἵνα τὰ μαχρὰν >> ὑπάρχοντα ἔθνη πρὸς σωτηρίαν შაბ. τὸ ἑαυτοῦ φῶς καλέσῃ. კურთხევათა მათ თ»ს იაკობისთა გამოთქუმად სიტყუანი. მეო»ფლისა მიერ კურთხევათა მათ ვემიებ. რამეთუ ვინმე არს კაცთაგანი შემმლებელ გამოთქუმად სულიერთა წიგნთა გან. არა თუ მისა ზესკნელი იგი სიბრმნე თანაშემწშ საქმითა იყოს ანუ ვინ შემმლებელ არს გამოთქუმად დაწერილნი იგი ნეტართა წინაისწარმეტყუელთანი იგავით გამოუჩინებელნი სიტყუანი. არა თუ თთ სიტყუამან მან გამოთქუას ვითარ წინანდელთა მათ 984ხ "თს იL4სიLIმსისყს მოლი თი სხ დსთ, ხი ცთნიის თLც6სიL/მნ.სთს ვ ----_ოაოაოძოძზჩტ”'””'“' “_-_ ხმს τς სმი სენით თს სმითი ββμεῖάι დილბითჩყ სვნ ჩის პხ(სნ, ლიე ნ" გხიხხიყ ნიის კხტისნეს მიილვიო,სეშ თი იC ხC. αὔιϊμέτε πα μιωϊιίναιν, ხ90ხ იჯ საცხს დ ცნს ცს მსსნიყნ, ოლ კროსი გჩხდიყნეს, ოლრყნს ἄμε μἷμρ ται ρ βῆναι ρῖν, სიენ+C ცი ფლინის, ბიბ ნი ხდ M ს ყხიტეტილიიყ იის ხხტნეს მყსთთი. მნი ჟამთა ისრატლსა შორის. ვითარცა სანთელი შჯულსა შინა გჯმირსა ქჯეშე დამა- ლულ იყო. რაითა განმართლებულნი შჯულითა წინადაცუეთით განათლებად შეუმლოს. და აწ განცხადებულად ზედა მელსა ვითარცა სასანთლესა შ»დ პირად სანთელნი აღნთებულ და გამოჩინებულ. რაითა შორიელთაცა ნათესავთა ცხო– რებად თუსსა მას ნათელსა მოუწოდეს:. At the moment when, upon the blessings of Jacob, I undertake to discourse (1), I ask myself for the blessing (2) which comes to us from the Lord (3). Who, in effect, among men is capable of narrating (4) that which was said in a spiritual sense, if the celestial Wisdom does not collaborate with him while he speaks? And (5) who can interpret that which was said by the blessed prophets by means of parables, in covered words, if the Word himself does not make himself his own interpreter? (This Word) who, in the ancient days first, in Israel, like a lamp, in the time of the Law, under the bushel was hidden in order to illuminate those who were, in the time of the Law, justified by means of circumcision; then (Word which), in the time where we are, in a language which says everything, appears on the wood, like a lamp with seven branches on a candlestick, in order to call to salvation, to his own light, even to the distant nations (6).

### VISIONS OF JOSEPH

XL0თ0VM6 Παρέστω τοίνυν ἡμῖν αὐτὸς ὁ λόγος ἑρμηνεὺς τῶν ἑαυτοῦ μυστηρίων γινόμενος, ἵνα καὶ τὰ τοῦ Ἰωσὴφ ὁράματα τοῖς πιστεύουσιν φανερῶσαι δυνηθῇ. οὐδὲ γὰρ μάτην τοῦτον ὁραματιστὴν ἐκάλουν οἱ ἀδελφοί, εἰ μὴ δυνάμει προεώρων τὸν ἐν αὐτῷ πολιτευό- ΡΟ ρ. μενον λόγον. « εἶπον γὰρ πρὸς ἑαυτοὺς λέγοντες. ἰδοὺ ὁ ὁραματιστὴς ἔρχεται" δεῦτε καὶ ἀποχτείνωμεν αὐτόν, καὶ ἴδωμεν τί ἔσται τὰ ὁράματα αὐτοῦ ». τίνα δὲ ἦν τὰ τούτου ὁράματα, υἱοὶ ”IXXCთ6, εἴπατε. ἐθεώρει, φησίν, δράγματα ἐν πεδίῳ δεσμούμενα
(7) May he therefore assist us himself, the Word, making himself interpreter of his own mysteries, so that them too, the visions of Joseph, he might put his power to making them clear to the believers. And, in effect, it is not without reason that his brothers called him the visionary since they did not see in advance the Word, by power, making in him his dwelling. They said in effect, speaking to themselves: "Here comes the visionary! Come, let us therefore, and let us kill him, and let us see what will become of his visions!" But what were his visions, sons of Jacob, tell? -
καὶ τὸ ἑαυτοῦ δράγμα ὀρθούμενον, τὰ δὲ ἡμέτερα πεσόντα᾽ ἐπιστραφέντα δὲ προσεκύνουν τὸ τοῦ Ἰωσὴφ δράγμα. τί ἐν τούτῳ ἠδίκησεν ᾿Ιωσήφ, εἰ τὸ μέλλον ἔσεσθαι ἀπήγγειλεν ὑμῖν; τί παραζηλοῦτε καὶ μισεῖτε τὸν δίκαιον, εἰ θεὸς τούτῳ τὰ ἑαυτοῦ μυστήρια ἀπεκάλυψεν καὶ τὰ ἐπ’ ἐσχάτων γινόμενα δι᾽ ὁραμάτων ἐφανέρωσεν; τί ἐλυπήθητε ἰδόντες χιτῶνα ποικίλον ἐπ᾽ αὐτῷ, εἰ ὁ δίκαιος πατὴρ τοῦτον παρὰ πάντας ἀγαπήσας ἐτίμησεν καὶ ποιμένα ποιμένων εἰς ἐπίσκεψιν ὑμῖν ἀπέστειλεν καὶ μάρτυρα πιστὸν ἐν κόσμῳ προήγαγεν καὶ δράγμα γήρως ὡς ἀπαρχὴν ἅγιον πρωτότοχον ἐκ νεχρῶν ἀνέστησεν; τί ἀγαναχτεῖτε, εἰ ἥλιος καὶ σελήνη καὶ ἕνδεκα ἀστέρες προσεκύνουν αὐτόν, οἱ πάλαι προτυπούμενοι; οὐδὲ Ἰακὼδ καὶ ῬῬαχὴλ ἥλιος καὶ σελήνη ἐχρημάτιζεν, ἀλλ᾽ οὐδὲ γὰρ οὕτω γεγένηται. ἡ μὲν γὰρ Ῥαχὴλ ἤδη τεθνεῶσα ἐν τῷ ἱπποδρόμῳ ἐτέθαπτο, ὁ δὲ ᾿Ιωσὴφ συναντήσας τῷ πατρὶ αὑτοῦ, πεσὼν προσεκύνησεν, ἵνα ὁ τύπος τηρηθῇ καὶ ἡ Ὑ რამეთუ იგინი სახედ ხოლო არამედ იაკობ და რაქელ არა თუ მზს და მთოვარტ0 იყვნეს. და არცაღა თუ ესე ესრმ ყოფილ არს რამეთუ რაქელ მრა– კლით ჟამითგან გზასა მას დამარხული დგას ხოლო იოსებ ვითარ შეემთხჯა სიიყს ს ხღტიტიმნთნ. I იგ ნ9ნ ნიც ჩL ჩინ, ინით ნ ხL μπεωβῖν
სს. ხC იგ ყითL ი ინს სენი ნ, ცისის Πμαρ μι «იდე მხი ნი ' გსსიტნსნს მითენ რ მო ინანა იყონ. კიიი ს მნი ნის. ხ- იყიდე გნ μι ნოი ს რირნიყედ δα გდ ὠρημαρευφίκωι ხელნა ნივ: სღ, ჩად ἱμωιπιωρβ ხავის აბია კამის ავბა ათ ს/მ ყვევეყათ ან აბიალი პი საბის მიეს μία ᾧ' ἡνία γραφὴ ἀλχηθεύουσα φανῇ. ποῦ οὖν πληροῦται τὸ εἰρημένον, τὸ « ἄρά γε ἐλθόντες ἐγὼ καὶ ἡ μήτηρ σου καὶ οἱ ἀδελφοί σου προσκυνήσωμέν σοι ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς », ἀλλ᾽ ἢ ὅτε οἱ μακάριοι ἀπόστολοι ἅμα ᾿Ιωσὴφ καὶ Μαρίᾳ ἐλθόντες ἐν τῷ ὄρει τῶν ἐλαιῶν προσεκύνησαν τὸν Χριστόν; ᾿Ιούδας μετ᾽ αὐτῶν οὐχ ἦν. οὐδὲ γὰρ ὁ Ἰωσὴφ αὐτὸς Χριστὸς ἦν, ἵνα ἐν Αἰγύπτῳ τοῦτο γεγενῆσθαι νομισθῇ, ἀλλὰ ἦν τύπος τοῦ μέλλοντος παραδεικνυμένου διὰ Ἰωσήφ᾽ παρέστω τοίνυν ὁ ἀληθινὸς καὶ ἐπουράνιος Ιωσὴφ ἑρμηνεύς, ἵνα τὰ δι᾽ αὐτοῦ κηρυχθέντα ὑπὸ τῶν μακαρίων προφητῶν αὐτὸς πάλιν ἑρμηνεύων φανῇ᾽ οὐδὲ γὰρ παρεσιώπησεν ἡ γραφὴ ἐπὶ τοῦ Ἰωσήφ, ἀλλὰ ἑρμηνέα τοῦτον ἀπέφηνεν᾽ αὐτὸς γὰρ ἦν ἑρμηνεὺς τῶν τοῦ πατρὸς ἀποχρύφων μυστηρίων. καὶ γὰρ γραφὴ λέγει" « αὐτοὶ δὲ οὐκ ἔγνωσαν ὅτι ἀκούει » αὐτῶν « ὁ Ἰωσήφ᾽ ὁ γὰρ ἑρμηνεὺς ἀνὰ μέσον αὐτῶν ἦν ». მამასა Τ᾽ ხ პირველად მას თაყუანის-სცა. რაითა სახ0 იგი ეგოს და წიგნნი განმართლე ბულ იპოვნენ აწ სადაღა აღესრულების თქუმული იგი. აწ უკუე მო-სამე-ვიდეთ მე და დედაი შენი და შმანი შენნი და თაყუანისგცემდეთ
said, namely: "Will it therefore be that we must come I and your mother and your brothers, to worship you front against earth?" if not when (13) the blessed Apostles, together with Joseph and Mary, once arrived on the Mount of Olives, worshipped Christ? Judas was not with them (14). And Joseph, in effect, was not Christ in person, to the point that one would go to believe that this (prophetic vision) was realized in Egypt, but he was the figure of the (Christ) to come, represented by Joseph (15). (16) May he come therefore the true and celestial Joseph, as interpreter, so that, that which, through his intermediary, was announced by the blessed Prophets, He himself, again, by interpreting it, might make himself seen! (17) And, in effect, the Scripture has not (thereupon) kept silence in the history of Joseph, but it has well made us see it (18), Him (i. e. the celestial Joseph), as interpreter. He himself in effect was interpreter of the hidden mysteries of the Father. And, in effect, the Scripture says: "For them, they did not perceive (19) that Joseph understood them, for the interpreter was between them intermediary."

"He contemplated, he says, in a plain, sheaves bound; and his own sheaf stood upright, but ours had fallen; then having turned toward it, they worshipped the sheaf of Joseph." What rights, in the occurrence, has Joseph violated, if he announced to you there that which was to be? Why do you envy and hate the just one, if God revealed to him his own mysteries, and if he made clear by visions the events of the end of times? Why your faces saddened at the sight of a tunic embroidered on his back if the Just Father (8), in his love preferring him to all, honored him, and sent you to visit a Shepherd of shepherds, and produced in the world a faithful witness and, sheaf of old age (9), like first fruits, caused to rise from among the dead a holy First-Born? Why be indignant if the sun and the moon and eleven stars worshipped him? They are there, from ancient times, as prefigurators (10). And neither was Jacob called (11) "Sun", nor Rachel, "Moon". For also well things did not happen thus. Rachel, she, in effect, already dead, lay buried in the hippodrome. As for Joseph, when he had gone to the meeting of his father, having prostrated himself, he worshipped so that the value of type might be well kept and that the Scripture might appear well saying the truth (12). said, namely: "Will it therefore be that we must come I and your mother and your brothers, to worship you front against earth?" if not when (13) the blessed Apostles, together with Joseph and Mary, once arrived on the Mount of Olives, worshipped Christ? Judas was not with them (14). And Joseph, in effect, was not Christ in person, to the point that one would go to believe that this (prophetic vision) was realized in Egypt, but he was the figure of the (Christ) to come, represented by Joseph (15). (16) May he come therefore the true and celestial Joseph, as interpreter, so that, that which, through his intermediary, was announced by the blessed Prophets, He himself, again, by interpreting it, might make himself seen! (17) And, in effect, the Scripture has not (thereupon) kept silence in the history of Joseph, but it has well made us see it (18), Him (i. e. the celestial Joseph), as interpreter. He himself in effect was interpreter of the hidden mysteries of the Father. And, in effect, the Scripture says: "For them, they did not perceive (19) that Joseph understood them, for the interpreter was between them intermediary."
γραφὴ ἀληθεύουσα φανῇ. ποῦ οὖν πληροῦται τὸ εἰρημένον, τὸ « ἄρά γε ἐλθόντες ἐγὼ καὶ ἡ μήτηρ σου καὶ οἱ ἀδελφοί σου προσκυνήσωμέν σοι ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς », ἀλλ᾽ ὅτε οἱ μακάριοι ἀπόστολοι ἅμα Ἰωσὴφ καὶ Μαρίᾳ ἐλθόντες ἐν τῷ ὄρει τῶν ἐλαιῶν προσεκύνησαν τὸν Χριστόν; ᾿Ιούδας μετ᾽ αὐτῶν οὐκ ἦν. οὐδὲ γὰρ ὁ Ἰωσὴφ αὐτὸς Χριστὸς ἦν, ἵνα ἐν Αἰγύπτῳ τοῦτο γεγενῆσθαι νομισθῇ, ἀλλὰ ἦν τύπος τοῦ μέλλοντος παραδεικνυμένου διὰ Ἰωσήφ᾽ παρέστω τοίνυν ὁ ἀληθινὸς καὶ ἐπουράνιος Ιωσὴφ ἑρμηνεύς, ἵνα τὰ δι᾽ αὐτοῦ κηρυχθέντα ὑπὸ τῶν μακαρίων προφητῶν αὐτὸς πάλιν ἑρμηνεύων φανῇ" οὐδὲ γὰρ παρεσιώπησεν γραφὴ ἐπὶ τοῦ Ἰωσήφ, ἀλλὰ ἑρμηνέα τοῦτον ἀπέφηνεν' αὐτὸς γὰρ ἦν ἑρμηνεὺς τῶν τοῦ πατρὸς ἀποχρύφων μυστηρίων. καὶ γὰρ γραφὴ λέγει" « αὐτοὶ δὲ οὐκ ἔγνωσαν ὅτι ἀκούει » αὐτῶν « ὁ Ἰωσήφ᾽ ὁ γὰρ ἑρμηνεὺς ἀνὰ μέσον αὐτῶν ἦν ». მამასა I ხ პირველად მას თაყუანის–სცა. რაითა სახტ იგი ეგოს და წიგნნი განმართლე ბულ იპოვნენ აწ სადაღა აღესრულების თქუმული იგი. აწ უკუე მო-სამე-ვიდეთ მე და დედაი შენი და შმანი შენნი და თაყუანის- გცემდეთ შენ. არამედ რაჟამს ათორმეტნი იგი ნეტარნი მოციქულნი იოსებით– ურთ და მარიამით–ურთ აღვიდეს მთასა მას ზეთის-ხილთასა და თაყუანის- სცეს ქრისტესა რამეთუ იუდა არა მათ თანა იყო არცა იოსები თავადი ქრისტე იყო. რაითა ეგჯპტეს შინა ესე ყოფილად შერაცხილ იყოს. არამედ მსგავს იყო ყოფადთა მათ თს სახეთა. რომელ წინაისწარ გამოეცხადა იოსების გან წარ– მოდეგინ აწ საშუვალ ჭეშმარიტი იგი და ზეცისაი განმმარტებელი რამეთუ რომელ იგი მის მიერ იქადაგა ნეტართა მათ წინაისწარმეტყუელთა მიმართ მანვე მეორედ გამოაცხადენ არამედ არავე გარე წარჰტდეს წიგნნი იოსების გან. ვითარმცა არა განმმარტებელ ითქუა. რამეთუ იგი იყო გამომთხრობელ მამისა თს დაფარულთა მათ ზ%რახ Τ' ἃ ვათა. და წიგნნი იტყჯან. ვითარმედ მათ არა უწყოდეს ვითარმცა ესმა მათი იოსებს. რამეთუ განმმარტებელად შორის მათსა დგა:. soiled the tunic, the embroidered tunic! with the blood of that kid (22), lying and misinterpreting the events of which you yourselves were the authors (23)? The father, for his part, agreed with you in recognizing that it was indeed the son's tunic and endured (with pain) the event (24). And you, Judah, you sell Joseph, conquered as you are by "the concupiscence of the world." And the foreign Ishmaelites buy him so that he may be led into Egypt and that there he may be put in irons and that, of visions, the hidden meaning may be, through him, unveiled and that he may be, by Pharaoh, summoned and that he may be instituted King of Egypt and sit, in testimony of trust, on the second throne (25); and that wheat, in abundance, may be gathered by him and kept in store; and that the week of years may be accomplished; and that the starving people may be fed and that he may be, by his brothers, adored, and recognized by them as father (26). What did the brothers gain by then killing a kid and deceiving their father (20)? For behold, Joseph, in Egypt, has received the title (21) of king. Why have you

soiled the tunic, the embroidered tunic! with the blood of that kid (22), lying and misinterpreting the events of which you yourselves were the authors (23)? The father, for his part, agreed with you in recognizing that it was indeed the son's tunic and endured (with pain) the event (24). And you, Judah, you sell Joseph, conquered as you are by "the concupiscence of the world." And the foreign Ishmaelites buy him so that he may be led into Egypt and that there he may be put in irons and that, of visions, the hidden meaning may be, through him, unveiled and that he may be, by Pharaoh, summoned and that he may be instituted King of Egypt and sit, in testimony of trust, on the second throne (25); and that wheat, in abundance, may be gathered by him and kept in store; and that the week of years may be accomplished; and that the starving people may be fed and that he may be, by his brothers, adored, and recognized by them as father (26).

πλανήσαντες; ἰδοὺ γὰρ ὁ Ἰωσὴφ ἐν Αἰγύπτῳ βασιλεὺς ἐχρημάτισεν. τί ἐμολύνατε τὸν χιτῶνα τὸν ποικίλον τῷ αἵματι τοῦ ἐρίφου ψευδόμενοι καὶ παρερμηνεύοντες τὰ სდ” ὑμῶν γεγενημένα; ὁ δὲ πατὴρ χιτῶνα υἱοῦ τοῦτον εἶναι ὡμολόγει μεθ᾽ ὑμῶν καὶ ἔφερεν τὸ γεγενημένον. σὺ δὲ πωλεῖς, Ἰούδα, τὸν Ἰωσὴφ ἐπιθυμίᾳ κόσμου κρατούμενος. οἱ δὲ ἀλλόφυλοι Ἰσμαηλῖται τοῦτον ἀγοράζουσιν, ἵνα εἰς Αἴγυπτον ἀχθῇ κἀκεῖ δέσμιος γένηται καὶ ὁράματα δι᾿ αὐτοῦ ἀποκαλυφθῇ καὶ ὑπὸ Φαραῶ ζητηθῇ καὶ βασιλεὺς Αἰγύπτου κατασταθῇ καὶ τὸν δεύτερον θρόνον πισ τευθῇ καὶ σῖτος πολὺς δι᾽ αὐτοῦ συναχθῇ καὶ εἰς ἀποθήκην τηρηθῇ καὶ ი ἑδδομὰς ἐτῶν πληρωθῇ καὶ λαὸς λιμώττων τραφῇ καὶ ὑπὸ ἀδελφῶν προσχυνηθῇ καὶ πατὴρ ὑπ᾽ αὐτῶν ὁμολογηθῇ. იგიმცა თჯ;სი შეიტყუვეს აჰა აწ იოსებ ეგჯპტეს ცხოველ არს რაისა შეჰბღა- ლეთ სისხლითა სამოსელი იგი ყუავილოვანი ტყუვილით. რომელ იგი თქუენ ჰქმენით. რომელ იგი იაკობ მამამან იცნა სამოსელი იგი და ყოფილად შეგირაც– ხა განჰყიდი იუდა იოსებსა გულის-თქუმითა ამის სოფლისაითა მლეულ და ისმაიტელთა მათ უცხო-თესლთა მიიყიდეს. რაითა ეგჯპტედ შთავიდეს და მუნ საპყრობილედ მიეცეს და სიზმარნი მის მიერ განცხადნენ. რაითა ფარა–- ოის მიერ მოიშიოს და მეფედ მეგ»პტელთა გამოჩნდეს და შემდგომ მოსაყდრტ სარწმუნო გამოჩნდეს და იფქლი მის მიერ დიდმალად შეკრბეს და საუნჯეთა დაიმარხოს და შ»დნი იგი წელნი აღესრულნენ Τ Ὁ და ერი სიყმილისა მის გან გამოიზარდოს და შმათა გან თაყუანის-ცემაი მიიღოს და უფალ მათ მიერ იწოდოს:. ΡΟ გუჩუენნა არამედ რამეთუ ჟამი ესე გამოემიებს კურთხევათა მათ თვს განმარ– ტებასა. გულს-მოდგინებითა გონებისაითა ზედა-მცა-მიწევნულ ვართ. რაითა
(28), our attention once well fixed, let us see if the blessings pronounced by Jacob truly went, as blessings, upon the sons of Israel. For we find the opposite, namely: on the one hand, blessings, which there (in Egypt or in the Bible) are indeed such by formulation; on the other hand, proof of their (the sons of Israel's) guilt, and, presented in their persons, a very ample prophecy which was realized in the lineage of those who sprang from their stock. So much so that the blessed Jacob was speaking, it is true, to his own sons, as if it were a matter of the present, but what he was saying happened in the last times. And, on the one hand, beforehand, he distinguished the blessings by means of which these things (to come) were prophesied; on the other hand, he gave them to the sons of Israel, while proving their transgressions, and he threatened them, withdrawing from such actions (29). How then shall I not glorify the Heavenly King, he who, by using prophets, has staged (27) his own mysteries? Furthermore, since an opportunity has also arisen to turn to the interpretation of the Blessings

(28), our attention once well fixed, let us see if the blessings pronounced by Jacob truly went, as blessings, upon the sons of Israel. For we find the opposite, namely: on the one hand, blessings, which there (in Egypt or in the Bible) are indeed such by formulation; on the other hand, proof of their (the sons of Israel's) guilt, and, presented in their persons, a very ample prophecy which was realized in the lineage of those who sprang from their stock. So much so that the blessed Jacob was speaking, it is true, to his own sons, as if it were a matter of the present, but what he was saying happened in the last times. And, on the one hand, beforehand, he distinguished the blessings by means of which these things (to come) were prophesied; on the other hand, he gave them to the sons of Israel, while proving their transgressions, and he threatened them, withdrawing from such actions (29).

τοῦ Ἡσαῦ σημαίνει τὴν διὰ τοῦ λόγου κλῆσιν τοῦ προτέρου λαοῦ γεγενημένην, ὡς ἀπαιτεῖ καρπὸν ἔργων δικαιοσύνης παρ᾽ αὐτοῦ, ἥτις ἦν ὡς τροφὴ τῷ πατρὶ λογιζομένη. τὸ γὰρ « ἔξελθε εἰς τὸ πεδίον καὶ θήρευσόν μοι θήραν » τὴν ἐν τῷ κόσμῳ ἀναστροφὴν σημαίνει. τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν πρὸς αὐτόν, « λάθε τὴν φαρέτραν καὶ τὸ τόξον σου» δηλοῖ, ὅτι ἔμελλεν ὁ λαὸς ἰδίας δόξης ἀντιποιούμενος μὴ πίστει δικαιοῦσθαι, ἀλλ᾽ ἐν πολέμῳ καὶ გმა-ყო და არა აკურთხა. არამედ მლიერებითა. თქუმულთა მისთაითა დასაჯა
be justified by faith, but, glorying in war and the sword, ask for a king (who will be) their tyrant (34); as Moses, he too, said to them: "... and the sword is what you glory in (35)." in advance the wicked calculation that would come into his heart, did not bless him [in his turn], but, in the strength of the term, by a prophecy, he reprimanded him. Moreover, that it is indeed so, Scripture itself once opened will teach us more clearly what was said previously (30). Thus, indeed, it speaks: "And Isaac called his eldest son and said to him: 'Take your equipment: your quiver and your bow, and go out into the plain, and kill me some game, and make me a good dish, as I like it, and bring it to me, that I may eat, so that my soul may bless you before I die.'" The fact, therefore, that, on the one hand, the prophet, asking Esau for food, does so by giving him an order (31), signifies the call that the Word makes the first people hear when he asks them for the fruit of the works of justice, (justice) which was considered as food for the Father (32). For these words: "Go out into the plain and kill me some game" designate life in the world. The fact, on the other hand, of saying to him: "Take your quiver and your bow" shows that the people were to, claiming (33) their personal glory, not at all

"Now Rebecca said to Jacob, her younger son: 'Behold, I have just heard your father speaking to Esau, your brother, and saying to him: "Bring me some of your game, and make me a dish, and, after having eaten, I will bless you before the Lord." Now therefore, listen to my voice, my child, and, having gone to the sheepfold, take me from there two little kids (36), tender and beautiful; and I will make a dish of them for your father, as he likes it, and you will bring it to your father, and he will eat of it; so that your father may bless you before he dies.'" Now therefore, listen to my voice, my child, and, having gone to the sheepfold, take me from there two little kids (36), tender and beautiful; and I will make a dish of them for your father, as he likes it, and you will bring it to your father, and he will eat of it; so that your father may bless you before he dies.'"

On the one hand, therefore, Rebecca, the image-bearer of the Church, was already prefiguring (there) the future that was to be realized through her younger son. For she says to him: "Having gone to the sheepfold, take me from there two little kids, tender and beautiful." Esau, for his part, is sent into the plain as living as a stranger (37) in the world; but Jacob (is sent) to the sheepfold, so that what was said by the Lord may be accomplished: "I was not sent except to the lost sheep of the House of Israel." On the one hand, therefore, Rebecca, the image-bearer of the Church, was already prefiguring (there) the future that was to be realized through her younger son. For she says to him: "Having gone to the sheepfold, take me from there two little kids, tender and beautiful." Esau, for his part, is sent into the plain as living as a stranger (37) in the world; but Jacob (is sent) to the sheepfold, so that what was said by the Lord may be accomplished: "I was not sent except to the lost sheep of the House of Israel."

On the other hand, from the fact that she said: "Take me from there two little kids, tender and beautiful," she indicated (thereby) the two calls which, in fact, are found to have been addressed by the Gospel. On the one hand, being kids at the beginning (38), since we were all sinners, we become on the other hand, through obedience, tender and beautiful (39), justified by faith in Christ, no longer as condemned kids but, as sheep, in pure sacrifice, in "odor of sweetness" offered to God <and constituted spiritual food for the Word> (40) who, by means of the Gospel, giving the similitudes their full realization, said to his disciples: "I have, myself, food to eat, which you do not know."

"ი 6იმილსინ, ხყენC იყMMტნ სი ჩL ყი6 ხიხუ სხთი:Cბი, მიი-ხიც, ხL ს ხდის. φπηΐνε πρὶ ფისს ნინიი ნიის, ი ἔτεα. გრი ნოხი დიის ხის მიმი ომის რიან ხსნი ვისის ნლითს” სმს სა ἰμημαίμιερ πεῖ μὴ, გოდ ოხ ი: ἡβιιξῃ V. «Εἶπεν δὲ Ἰακὼδ πρὸς τὴν μητέρα αὐτοῦ’ ἔστιν ὁ ἀδελφός μου ἀνὴρ δασύς, ἐγὼ δὲ ἀνὴρ λεῖος. μήποτε ψηλαφήσῃ με ὁ πατήρ μου καὶ ἔσομαι ἐναντίον αὐτοῦ ὡς κατα- φρονῶν καὶ ἐπάξω ἐπ᾽ ἐμαυτὸν κατάραν καὶ οὐκ εὐλογίαν.» πῶς σαφῶς ἐνθάδε τὸ ἴδιον εὐλαδὲς ὁ Ἰακὼδ ἐπέδειξεν. μαθὼν γὰρ τὸ γεγενημένον ὑπὸ τοῦ Χάμ, ὃς ἰδὼν γυμνωθέντα τὸν Νῶε κατεγέλασεν τὴν γύμνωσιν τοῦ πατρὸς αὐτοῦ καὶ ἐπεσπάσατο ბდ” ἑαυτὸν τὴν κατάραν, და იაკობ ე ტყოდა რებეკას დედასა თჯ»სსა. ვითარმედ არს მშმაი ჩემი მატყიერ და მე კაცი წყრტუ ვარ ნუ უკუე მრევდეს მამაი ჩემი. და ვიპოვო
But Jacob said to Rebecca his mother: "My brother is (41) a hairy man, and I, a smooth man. (I fear) that my father may come to feel me, and I will appear (42) in his eyes to despise him, and I will bring upon myself a curse and not a blessing." How (43) clearly here Jacob has indeed shown his own piety! For instructed in what had happened through the fault of Ham, who, having seen Noah naked, had mocked the nakedness of his father, and had brought the curse upon himself, he took good care not to go and risk himself too (44) of passing for someone


[p. 17] {#po.27.p17}
მის წინაშე ვითარცა შეურაცხის-მყოფელი და მოვიკრიბო თავისა ჩემისა წყევაი და არა კურთხევაი ჰხედავა რამეთუ განცხადებულად თ»სი იგი მოშიშებაი იაკობ გამოაჩინა I ἃ მეცნიერ ყოფილ საქმეთა მათ ქამისთა. რომელ იხილა შიშულობაი იგი ნოვესი მამისა თ»სისაი და განიცინნა. რომლისა თ»სცა დაიკრიბა თავსა თ»სსა ზედა წყევაი იგი. შეეშინა იაკობსცა. ნუ უკუე ვითარცა ხნ. მისი სთ ენ ἡδίων ხ+ი, M9ხ < წ >> ხილის μὲ ი მი, ნ. ხი
trying to deceive his own father and falling under a curse. Furthermore, what is said (there) by him was true also, namely: "Esau my brother, he is a hairy man," that is to say, a sinner, "but I am, myself, a smooth man," by which is shown (the character) that the flesh of the Lord has of being without stain and without sin. But since it was not necessary that the mysteries, upon another (than the Lord? Jacob?), be accomplished (45), (Rebecca) says to him (46): "Upon me (be) your curse, my child (47); only be obedient to my voice, and go, bring me"

Καὶ γὰρ ἔστιν ἐπιγνῶναι τὸ εἰρη μένον ὑπὸ τῆς 'Ρεδέκκας πάλαι, νῦν ἐπὶ τὴν ἐκ- κλησίαν πεπληρωμένον. τὸ γὰρ « ἐπ᾽ ἐμὲ ἡ κατάρα σου τέκνον », τοῦτο δηλοῖ, ὅπερ νῦν τινες βλασφημοῦντες τὴν ἐκκλησίαν ὀνειδίζουσιν ὡς σεδομένην τὸν ἐσταυρωμένον, κατάραν ἡμῖν καὶ ὄνειδος κατὰ ταῦτα ἐπιφέροντες. τὸ γὰρ πάθος τοῦ κυρίου τοῖς μὲν ἀπίστοις νομίζεται χατάρα, τοῖς δὲ πιστεύουσιν « ζωὴ καὶ εἰρήνη ». καὶ γὰρ ὁ ἀπόστολος « Χριστὸς ἡμᾶς ἐξηγόρασεν ἐκ τῆς κατάρας τοῦ νόμου, γενόμενος ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν κατάρα», ὅπερ ἐπεπλέρωσεν ὁ და არს გულის-კმის-საყოფელ თქუმული იგი რებეკაის მიერ წინაისწარ.
For one can recognize that what, long ago, was said by Rebecca has now been accomplished upon the Church. For: "Upon me (be) your curse, my child," this makes manifest what precisely some now, in their blasphemies, outrageously reproach the Church with, of venerating the Crucified, adding on this subject against us curse and outrage (48). For (49) the Passion of the Lord is, on one side, for the infidels, deemed a curse, on the other, for the faithful "life and peace." And, indeed, the apostle says: "Christ has redeemed us from the curse of the Law, having become a curse for us," which is precisely what

[On the one hand] (53), the act of clothing him in the robe signifies that the Word was to clothe himself in flesh; on the other hand, the act of putting the skins of the little kids around his arms shows that he took upon himself the sins of us all, once he had extended his hands and arms on the cross, as Isaiah also said: "He himself took upon himself our sins and bore our infirmities." The fact, moreover, that Jacob says to his father: "I have done one by one the things that you had told me to do," signifies the obedience in which the Word stood day by day (54) vis-à-vis his Father; as also indeed in Ezekiel he says: "I did one by one the things that he had ordered me to do." Now Isaac said to him (to Jacob): "Who are you?" He said: "I am your son, your firstborn Esau (55)." It is not because he is ignorant that the prophet questions Jacob; for he contemplates in their spiritual significance the events. Joshua, son of Nun, questioning the Gibeonites was not indeed ignorant of what in their deceit they had schemed, but [knowingly] he forgave, so that [the Gentiles] coming to repentance and believing in Joshua and in Jesus might be saved. Thus, therefore, here too (in Genesis), must one understand (56).

"Approach me," said (Isaac), "and I will feel you, (my) child, (to see) if you are my son Esau, or not." And he approached him. And he, after having felt him, said: "The voice, indeed, is the voice of Jacob, but the hands are the hands of Esau." ΡΟ ρ. და ჰრქუა მას. მომეახლე და ამბორს–მიყავ შვილო. და ვითარცა მიეახლა, ამბორს–უყო მას და იყნოსა სული სამოსლისა მისისაი და აკურთხა იგი. და ჰრქუა ამის თ»ს წინაისწარ განცხადებულად οὐδε წინაისწარმეტყუელი იგი. ვითარმედ არავინ კაცთაგანმან ამბორს–უყოს მამასა გარნა მხოლოდ–შობილ– მან მან. რომელ ქალწულისა გან იშვა რამეთუ იტყ»ს. მე ვარ იგი შენი პირმშოი და მუნთქუესვე იწყო წინაისწარმეტყუელმან მან ერთობით კურ– თხევისა მის თ»ს. და თქუა. აჰა ესერა სულნელებაი სამოსლისა ძისა ჩემისაი ვითარცა სული აგარაკისა სავსისაი. რომელ აკურთხა ოჯფალმან და ჰრქუა მას. მოგეცინ შენ ღმერთმან ცუარისა გან ცისაი სა და სიპოხისა გან ქუვყანისაისა. სიმრ ავლ0 იფქლისა და ღა»ნისაი და გმონებდენ შენ ნათე– სავნი და თაყუანის-გცემდენ შენ კელმწიფენი. და იყო შენ უფალ ძმისა შენისა და თაყუანის–გცენ შენ შეთა მამისა შენისათა რომელმან გაკურთხოს შენ კურ– თხეულ იყავნ. და რომელმან გწყეოს შენ წყეულ იყავნ
He (Isaac) said to him then: "Approach me, (my) child, and kiss me." And, having approached, he (Jacob) kissed him. And he (Isaac) smelled the scent of his garments and blessed him and said. And, in saying this, the prophet clearly reveals in advance that no one among men will offer the Father a holy mouth, except, and alone, the first-born <son> (58bis), born of the Virgin. "I am, indeed, said (Jacob), your son, the first-born." Immediately then the prophet imposed his blessing upon him and said: "Behold the scent of my son's garments, <like> (59) the scent of a fertile field which the Lord has blessed! And may God give you, from the dew of heaven and the fat of the earth, abundance of grain and wine! And may the <Gentiles> be enslaved to you, <may> princes <adore you> (60)! And be the lord of your brother, and may the sons of your father adore you! Whoever curses you, may he be cursed! Whoever blesses you, may he be blessed!"

ἔτη δουλεύων τῷ Λάβαν, ἔπειτα αὐτὸς τὸν ἴδιον ἀδελφὸν Ἡσαῦ προσκυνῶν καὶ διὰ τῶν δώρων τὸ τούτου πρόσωπον ἐξιλασκόμενος, μετὰ ταῦτα πάλιν εἰς Αἴγυπτον κατερχόμενος, ἵνα μὴ σὺν τοῖς τέκνοις λιμῷ διαφθαρῇ. ἐπὶ τίνα οὖν πληροῦται τὸ εἰρημένον" « ἰδοὺ ὀσμὴ τῶν ἱματίων τοῦ υἱοῦ μου’ ὧς ὀσμὴ ἀγροῦ πλήρης, ὃν εὐλόγησεν κύριος»; ἐπ᾽ οὐδένα ἄλλον, εἰ μὴ ἐπὶ τὸν Χριστόν, τὸν υἱὸν τοῦ θεοῦ. ἀγρὸς γάρ ἐστιν ὁ κόσμος. ὀσμὴ δὲ τῶν ἱματίων αὐτοῦ εἰσιν πάντες οἱ πιστεύοντες εἰς αὐτόν. ὡς ὁ ἀπόστολος λέγει" « ὅτι Χριστοῦ εὐωδία ἐσμὲν ἐν τοῖς σωζομένοις καὶ ἐν τοῖς ἀπολλυμένοις, οἷς μὲν γὰρ ὀσμὴ θανάτου εἰς θάνατον, οἷς δὲ ὀσμὴ ζωῆς εἰς ζωήν ». თაყუანის–სცა და მშღუნითა პირსა "მისსა ევედრა. და ამისა შემდგომად ეგ»პტედ შთავიდა. რაითა არა ნაშობით–ურთ სიყმილითა მოწყდენ აწ ვის ზედა აღეს– რულების თქუმული იგი. ვითარმედ აჰა ესერა სულნელებაი მისა ჩემისაი ვი– თარცა სული აგარაკისა სავსისაი. რომელი აკურთხა ო»ფალმან. არავის ზედა. არამედ ქრისტეს ზედა მისა მის ღმრთისა აგარაკ არს სოფელი ესე. სულნე– ლებასა მას მოსლვისა მისისასა არიან ყოველთა რომელთა ჰრწამს იგი. თარცა მოციქული იგი იტყ»ს. ვითარმედ სულ Τ სულნელების ვართ ქრისტესა. ცხოველთა და მკუდართა რომელთათ;სმე სულად სიკუდილითი სიკუდილად. და რომელთათ;სმე სულად ცხორებითი ცხორებად -“-ოეე__-- _-–-– სიდი μη იკნიჟ ε ცხი. ხიდს ἐθηιωμηπω ნი, გს ბს ნახეს ილეთს სხლო6 ს იმი თია სრსვს + + μὰ რთიიტს იხიყნით/ს. ნიის ით 6თსენლბსე იღეის სმო ჩტო. გხოთ αὔωμη, ნიც, ოდ ახლტსხითე ინდ + Πξ კის «L ს ენითა ჯილეხს თხთიCბი, სყ ნ «გMMლნი, 6ით 6თსინეპსე სიღ ხს «მნსხცხითს, ილ ს რიიხიიიის, იდი ნი სითი ხნ 9C /ენთთითს ით სი ხმ ს სხსისხს κε ს სიცინთნიი. იმისე ით ს მინით; ს მ.6, δὲ. იმისყ ით სხსისი+./ძხს ჩ ნით Ζ΄. Τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν" « δῴη σοι ὁ θεὸς ἀπὸ τῆς δρόσου τοῦ οὐρανοῦ καὶ ἀπὸ τῆς πιότη- τος τῆς γῆς πλῆθος σίτου καὶ οἴνου » σαφέστατα διὰ τοῦ ῥητοῦ τὸν λόγον, τὸν ἀπὸ οὐρανῶν χκαταθάντα ὡς δρόσον, ἐσήμανεν' τὴν δὲ γῆν, τὴν σάρκα, τὴν ἐκ τῆς παρθένου προσ- εἰλημμένην. « πλῆθος δὲ σίτου καὶ οἴνου » εἰπὼν ἔδειξεν τοὺς ἁγίους ὡς σῖτον εἰς ἀποθήκην συναγομένους καὶ διὰ τοῦ πνεύματος ὡς οἴνῳ δικαιουμένους. ხოლო რომელ იგი თქუა. მოგეცინ შენ ოჯფალმან ცუარისა გან ზეცისაისა და სიპოხისა გან ქუეყანისა. სიმრავლშ იფქლისა და ღ»ნისაი. განცხადებულად თქუმულითა მით ვითარცა ცუარსა მოგუასწავებს სიტყუასა მას. რომელ გარდა– მოგდა ზეცით და რომელ თქუა. ვითარმედ სიპოხისა გან ქუეყანისაისა. გორ– ცთა მათ ოჯფლისათა οὗ. რომელ იგი ქალწულისა გან შთაიცუნა და რომელ იგი თქუა. სიმრავლტ იფქლისაი და ღჯნისაი. გჯჩუენებს სახესა წმიდა- თასა საუნჯეთა შეკრებულ და სულითა ვითარცა ღ»ნითა განმხიარულებულ μ᾽ განს შს თხენ ცხ თხთიხიბ ს ყირელ სესე იილიღთი/ ნ სეტს μωιηᾶπι მნის ვოღსნოჯ κ ἡβῖαι πρ μωμηϊκωαμει ბიიიენის ყრიან იდ კსოსხე ნი სტიხ+ გერ, სგისIნ L ს სლსსდ ηδωμᾶμῖάι, იდ ს ოინს ცირ + βιμηὅηι θεῖν ყიცხსის δι ἡβῖα τς თხვნთL" ἡμπιρριῆι ხეო, კიხმთღი < βρρίκ ყილხთს > ძიცი ναι L იყLიმს ჩლლსC ფMსხიი რ,ლერღთენი + Ζ΄. As for this saying: "May God give you, from the dew of heaven and the fat of the earth, abundance of grain and wine," very clearly, by the term (employed), he designated the Word, who descended from heaven like dew; and, by the earth, the flesh which he assumed from the Virgin; and, by saying: "Abundance of grain and wine," he showed the saints gathered like grain (65) in a storehouse and, thanks to the Spirit, [justified as if by wine] (66). As for this saying: "May God give you, from the dew of heaven and the fat of the earth, abundance of grain and wine," very clearly, by the term (employed), he designated the Word, who descended from heaven like dew; and, by the earth, the flesh which he assumed from the Virgin; and, by saying: "Abundance of grain and wine," he showed the saints gathered like grain (65) in a storehouse and, thanks to the Spirit, [justified as if by wine] (66).

Τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν’ « δουλουσάτωσάν σοι ἔθνη καὶ προσκυνήσουσίν σοι ἄρχοντες » << τοῦτο καὶ μεχρὶ νῦν γίνεται" τίνι σήμερον τὰ ἔθνη πιστεύοντα δουλεύουσι καὶ τίνα προσκυνοῦσιν οἱ τῆς ἐκκλησίας ἄρχοντες >> ἀλλ᾽ ἢ τὸν Χριστόν, ბ” οὗ ὀνόματος καὶ σώζονται. ὡς καὶ διὰ Ἡσαΐου αὐτὸς ὁ λόγος προαναφωνῶν λέγει « τοῖς δὲ δουλεύουσίν μοι κληθήσεται ὄνομα καινόν, ὃ εὐλογηθήσεται ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς" εὐλογήσουσιν γὰρ τὸν θεὸν τὸν ἀληθινὸν « καὶ οἱ ὁμνύοντες ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς ὁμοῦνται τὸν θεὸν τὸν ἀληθινόν >> ». καὶ πάλιν λέγει" «ἰδοὺ οἱ δουλεύοντές μοι φάγονται, ὑμεῖς δὲ πεινάσετε᾽ ἰδοὺ οἱ δουλεύοντές μοι πίονται, ὑμεῖς δὲ διψήσετε᾽ ἰδοὺ οἱ δουλεύοντές μοι ἀγαλλιάσονται ἐν εὐφροσύνῃ, ὑμεῖς δὲ αἰσχυνθήσεσθε, ἀπὸ συντριδῆς πνεύματος ὀλολύξετε ». და რამეთუ თქუა. ვითარმედ გმონებდენ შენ ნათესავნი და თაყუანისგცემდენ

As for this saying: "May the Gentiles be enslaved to you! And may princes adore you," <this, even until this hour, is being realized. Who, today, do the Gentiles, having become believers, serve? And who do the princes of the Church adore> (67), if not Christ, by whose name they are also saved? As also, through Isaiah, the Word himself, announcing it in advance, says: "Those who serve me will be called by a new name, which will be blessed upon the earth. For they will bless the true God; <and whoever swears by the heavens, swears (henceforth) by the true God> (68)." And again he says: "Behold, those who serve me will eat; but you, you will be hungry; behold, those who serve me will drink; but you, you will be thirsty; behold, those who serve me will exult with joy; but you, you will be in shame; in the tearing of your spirit you will howl."

Without any doubt, the dish of Esau designates the worship of the people under the law, in that (this people) boasted and considered themselves to be justified by circumcision. They offered (to God) the Gentile proselytes as food, lacking food themselves, not being able to touch the heavenly bread (71). Without any doubt, the dish of Esau designates the worship of the people under the law, in that (this people) boasted and considered themselves to be justified by circumcision. They offered (to God) the Gentile proselytes as food, lacking food themselves, not being able to touch the heavenly bread (71).
ΡΟ Εἴπεν οὖν Ἡσαῦ" « ἀναστήτω ὁ πατήρ μου καὶ φαγέτω τῆς θήρας τοῦ υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ, ὅπως εὐλογήσῃ με ἡ ψυχή σου ». πῶς ὑπερήφανον τὸ ῥῆμα καὶ ἐπηρμένον τῇ καρδίᾳ" καὶ γὰρ ἕως σήμερον οἱ ἐκ περιτομῆς ὁμοίως καυχῶνται, ὡς μόνοι δεδικαιωμένοι. ὁ δὲ μακάριος Ἰακὼδ τὸ εὐλαδὲς καὶ ταπεινόφρον προσλαδόμενος ἔλεγεν τῷ πατρί" « ἐποίησα καθ᾽ ἃ ἐλάλησάς μοι»" ὁ δὲ ᾿Ησαῦ οὐχ οὕτως, ἀλλὰ « ἀναστήτω ὁ πατήρ μου καὶ φαγέτω τῆς θήρας τοῦ υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ, ὅπως εὐλογήσῃ με ψυχή σου ». ὁ δὲ πρὸς αὐτὸν ἔφη" «τίς εἴ σύ; ὁ δὲ εἴπεν' ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ υἱός σου, ὁ πρωτότοκος, ᾿Ησαῦ. ἐξέστη δὲ ᾿Ισαὰκ ἔκστασιν. μεγάλην σφόδρα καὶ εἴπεν᾽ τίς οὖν ὁ θηρεύσας μοι θήραν καὶ εἰσενέγκας μοι, καὶ ἔφαγον ἀπὸ πάντων πρὸ τοῦ σε ἐλθεῖν καὶ ηὐλόγησα αὐτόν, καὶ εὐλογημένος ἔσται ». πῶς ἐγρηγόρησεν ἐνθάδε τὸ πνεῦμα τοῦ προφήτου. ἐπειδὴ γὰρ ἐν τῇ εὐλογίᾳ προσομιλῶν τῷ Ἰακὼδ εἴπεν᾽ « ὁ εὐλογῶν σε εὐλογημένος" ὁ δὲ καταρώμενός σε ἐπικατάρατος», 0” διὰ τοῦτο ἐπεσφαλίσατο ἐκ δευτέ- ρου καὶ ἐπεσφράγισεν τὴν πρώτην εὐλογίαν εἰπών' « ηὐλόγησα αὐτὸν καὶ εὐλογημένος ἔσται ა. ἦν γὰρ τὸ γεγονὸς ὑπὸ τοῦ Ἰακὼδ μυστήριον οἰκονομίας εἰς Χριστὸν προτυπού- μενον, ὃς εὐλογημένος ὑπὸ τοῦ πατρὸς γεννηθεὶς εἰς τοὺς αἰῶνας ἐγένετο εὐλογημένος. ჰრქუა აღდეგ მამაო ჩემო და ჭამე. ნადირისა გან ძისა შენისაი და

ო0ძ0ძ”ძტქ0–––>____---ჟ----–_. სახენი ი განაკრძალა და მეორითა მით დაამტკიცა წინაისწარი იგი კურთხევვაი. და ჰრქუა. ვაკურთხე იგი და იყავნ კურთხეულ. და იყო ყოფილი იგი იაკობისგანი “რახვაი აღსრულებულ ქრისტესა ზედა წინაისწარ გამოხატულ. რომელი იკურთხა მამისა გან შობილ საუკუნითგან იყო კურთხეულ უკუე
Esau therefore said: "May my father rise and eat of his son's hunt; so that your soul may bless me." What pride in these words! And what arrogance in this heart! And, indeed, until today, those of the circumcision boast similarly, thinking they are the only ones to be justified. The blessed Jacob, for his part, joining piety and humility of spirit to it, said to his father: "I have done one by one (the things) that you told me (to do)." Esau, for his part, nothing of the sort, but: "May my father rise and eat of his son's hunt; so that your soul may bless me." Now (Isaac) said to him: "Who are you?" (Esau) said: "I am your son, your first-born, Esau." Then, beside himself, Isaac was greatly, very strongly, astonished, and said: "Who is it then who went to hunt game for me and brought it to me; and I ate of everything before you came; and I blessed him, and blessed he shall be." How the spirit of the prophet remained awake here! Because, indeed, in his blessing, addressing Jacob, he had said: "Whoever blesses you, may he be blessed! But whoever curses you, may he be cursed!" For this reason, he confirmed a second time and sealed the first blessing by saying: "I have blessed him, and blessed he shall be." What had indeed happened by the act of Jacob was a mystery of (divine) economy in view of Christ prefigured (by Jacob), (of Christ) who, blessed by the Father, once born (temporal birth), was blessed for the ages.

Τὸ δὲ εἰπεῖν τὴν γραφήν" « ἐξέστη ან σαῖς < რითაა μεγάλην σφόδρα », τοῦτ᾽ ἔστιν" ἐθαύμασεν Ἰσαὰκ > ἐπὶ τῷ γεγονότι, προορῶν τὰ >> ἔθνη διὰ τοῦ νεωτέρου υἱοῦ < ὑπὸ >> τοῦ θεοῦ μέλλοντα εὐλογεῖσθαι καὶ εἰς τὴν διαθήκην τῆς ἐπαγγελίας τῶν πατέρων χωρεῖν. διὰ τοῦτο γὰρ καὶ Ἰακὼδ τικτόμενος καὶ ἔσχατος ἐκ κοιλίας μητρὸς ἐκπορευόμενος τὴν πτέρναν ἐκράτει τοῦ Ἡσαῦ" ἤμελλεν γὰρ ὁ τοῖς ἴχνεσιν τῶν προφητῶν χατακολουθῶν ἔσχατος λαὸς λήψεσθαι τὰ πρωτοτόκια εἰς καινὴν διαθήκην πρῶτος εὑρισκόμενος. არამედ რამეთუ იტყუს წერილი იგი. ვითარმედ დაუკჯრდა ისაკსა დაკკრვე–
As for what Scripture says: "Then, beside himself, Isaac <was greatly, very strongly, astonished; this is the equivalent of: "Isaac was seized with admiration> (72)" regarding what had happened, seeing in advance that, the Gentiles, it was through the younger son that, <by> (73) God, they were to be blessed and to access the testament of the Promise made to the Fathers. It is for this, indeed, that Jacob even, at the moment when he was being born and was coming out last from his mother's womb, was gripping the heel of Esau. It is that, indeed, following closely in the tracks of the prophets, the last people was to seize the birthright, the first that one finds in the New Testament (74).

How manifestly here Scripture has predicted <through Esau the future events that arrived jointly>! And, indeed, the Word "Father of the people" was shown there, (Word) who, in the last times, thanks to the flesh (that he had taken) approached, even to touching it, the Passion (80). As Moses, making reproaches to the people, says: "Is this what you render to the Lord? Is He not your Father?" Scripture has therefore distinguished so that we might well understand what was said there. For the fact that Esau says: "When will the days approach when I will mourn my father?" is explained by the days of the Passover,

during which the Word [was approaching], preaching the Kingdom to them (81). But that Esau says: "So that I may kill Jacob, my brother," it is so that (thereby) might be shown the man himself (in the Word), the man who, according to the flesh, was begotten of Jacob, and, by him (Esau = the Jewish people) nailed to the wood. How manifestly here Scripture has predicted <through Esau the future events that arrived jointly>! And, indeed, the Word "Father of the people" was shown there, (Word) who, in the last times, thanks to the flesh (that he had taken) approached, even to touching it, the Passion (80). As Moses, making reproaches to the people, says: "Is this what you render to the Lord? Is He not your Father?" Scripture has therefore distinguished so that we might well understand what was said there. For the fact that Esau says: "When will the days approach when I will mourn my father?" is explained by the days of the Passover,

Esau (82) said to him then (to Isaac): "Have you not, besides, left a blessing for me, father?" Indeed, so that all the fullness of the divinity might be found dwelling bodily in Christ, this one (83) (Isaac) said to him: "If I have made him your Lord, and made all his brothers his servants, I have (at the same time) consolidated upon him (alone) the rights to the grain and to the oil; from then on, for you, what can I do, child? That was, therefore, the blessing that Esau was then asking to receive. But, because it was not the time, Isaac, prophesying the future, said to him: "Behold, from the fatness of the earth shall your dwelling be, and from the dew that descends from heaven (94). And by your sword you shall live, and you shall be your brother's servant. Yet a time will come when you will shake off and loosen the yoke from your neck." Indeed, does what the blessed Isaac says there have the value of a blessing or of a prophecy? For one must well understand what precedes. Behold, he says: "From the fatness of the earth shall your dwelling be, and from the dew that descends from heaven." It happened, in fact, that the people settled in the land of the Canaanites which Joshua, son of Nun, divided among them. And that Isaac says: "and from the

dew that descends from heaven" is because the prophets, like a cloud, left them soaked with dew (95) after having made known to them (96) the oracles of God. And that he says: "By your sword you shall live" is because, always, the people did not cease to be in aggressive and defensive war against the peoples established all around them, as the Scriptures themselves show. And what he says: "And you shall be your brother's servant" marks the era that still lasts now, (the era) when the Savior was present, having come to visit his own brothers according to the flesh, (the Savior) whom the prophet (here) advises, after one has become (97) docile, to serve. That is why he said: "Yet a time will come when you will shake off and loosen the yoke from your neck." What yoke if not the yoke that lies in the Law? So that they no longer live now as slaves under the yoke of the Law, but that, as free men believing in the Gospel, they may now still be saved (98). That was, therefore, the blessing that Esau was then asking to receive. But, because it was not the time, Isaac, prophesying the future, said to him: "Behold, from the fatness of the earth shall your dwelling be, and from the dew that descends from heaven (94). And by your sword you shall live, and you shall be your brother's servant. Yet a time will come when you will shake off and loosen the yoke from your neck." Indeed, does what the blessed Isaac says there have the value of a blessing or of a prophecy? For one must well understand what precedes. Behold, he says: "From the fatness of the earth shall your dwelling be, and from the dew that descends from heaven." dew that descends from heaven" is because the prophets, like a cloud, left them soaked with dew (95) after having made known to them (96) the oracles of God. And that he says: "By your sword you shall live" is because, always, the people did not cease to be in aggressive and defensive war against the peoples established all around them, as the Scriptures themselves show. And what he says: "And you shall be your brother's servant" marks the era that still lasts now, (the era) when the Savior was present, having come to visit his own brothers according to the flesh, (the Savior) whom the prophet (here) advises, after one has become (97) docile, to serve. That is why he said: "Yet a time will come when you will shake off and loosen the yoke from your neck." What yoke if not the yoke that lies in the Law? So that they no longer live now as slaves under the yoke of the Law, but that, as free men believing in the Gospel, they may now still be saved (98).

After having first given these indications on the blessings pronounced by Isaac, let us also come to the blessings (102) which are found in fact to have been, by Jacob himself, addressed to his sons. Thus, indeed, says the Scripture: "Now Israel, at the sight of the sons of Joseph, said: "Who are these?" (Joseph) said: "These are my sons, whom God has given me here." And (Jacob) said: "Bring (103) them to me, that I may bless them." Now the eyes of Jacob had become heavy because of old age, and could (104) no longer see well. And (Joseph) brought them near to him, and he kissed them and held them embraced (105). And Israel said to Joseph: "Behold, I have not been deprived of (seeing) your face, and behold, God has shown me even your lineage!" And Joseph withdrew them gently <from between

And Joseph having taken> (106) his two sons, Ephraim to his right, therefore to the left of Israel, and Manasseh to his left, therefore to the right of Israel, brought them near to (Jacob). Now Israel, having extended his right hand, placed it on the head of Ephraim - now he was the younger - and his left hand on the head of Manasseh. Having thus crossed his hands, he blessed them and said: "O God, before whom my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, were pleasing, O God who has been nourishing me from my youth until the day we are in, O Angel who delivered me from all my evils, bless (107) these little children, and upon them shall remain imposed my name and the name of my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, and may they multiply into a great multitude on the earth!" After having first given these indications on the blessings pronounced by Isaac, let us also come to the blessings (102) which are found in fact to have been, by Jacob himself, addressed to his sons. Thus, indeed, says the Scripture: "Now Israel, at the sight of the sons of Joseph, said: "Who are these?" (Joseph) said: "These are my sons, whom God has given me here." And (Jacob) said: "Bring (103) them to me, that I may bless them." Now the eyes of Jacob had become heavy because of old age, and could (104) no longer see well. And (Joseph) brought them near to him, and he kissed them and held them embraced (105). And Israel said to Joseph: "Behold, I have not been deprived of (seeing) your face, and behold, God has shown me even your lineage!" And Joseph withdrew them gently <from between
τὰς χεῖρας ηὐλόγησεν αὐτοὺς καὶ εἴπεν᾽ ὁ θεός, ᾧ εὐηρέστησαν οἱ πατέρες μου ἐναντίον αὐτοῦ, ᾿Αθραὰμ καὶ Ἰσαάκ, ὁ θεὸς ὁ τρέφων με ἐκ νεότητός μου ἕως τῆς σήμερον ἡμέρας, ὁ ἄγγελος, ὁ ῥυόμενός με ἐκ πάντων τῶν κακῶν μου, εὐλόγησον τὰ παιδία ταῦτα, καὶ ἐπιτεθήσεται τὸ ὄνομά μου ἐπ᾽ αὐτούς: καὶ τὸ ὄνομα τῶν πατέρων μου ᾿Αδραὰμ καὶ ᾿Ισαὰκ καὶ πληθυνθείησαν εἰς πλῆθος πολὺ ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς». the knees of (Jacob), and they worshiped him face to the ground. And Joseph having taken> (106) his two sons, Ephraim to his right, therefore to the left of Israel, and Manasseh to his left, therefore to the right of Israel, brought them near to (Jacob). Now Israel, having extended his right hand, placed it on the head of Ephraim - now he was the younger - and his left hand on the head of Manasseh. Having thus crossed his hands, he blessed them and said: "O God, before whom my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, were pleasing, O God who has been nourishing me from my youth until the day we are in, O Angel who delivered me from all my evils, bless (107) these little children, and upon them shall remain imposed my name and the name of my fathers, Abraham and Isaac, and may they multiply into a great multitude on the earth!"

[p. 48] {#po.27.p48}
ἰδιοποιήσατο τοὺς δύο υἱοὺς τοῦ Ἰωσήφ. τὸ γὰρ εἰπεῖν « οἱ δύο υἱοί, οἱ γενάμενοι ἐν Αἰγύπτῳ πρὸ τοῦ με ἐλθεῖν πρός σε εἰς Αἴγυπτον, ἐμοί εἰσιν, Ἐφραὶμ καὶ Μανασσῆς, ὡς Ῥουδὴμ καὶ Συμεὼν ἔσονταί μοι᾿ τὰ δὲ ἔγγονα, ἃ γεννήσεις μετὰ ταῦτα, σοὶ ἔσονται », τοῦτο σημαίνει: ἐπειδὴ γὰρ δώδεκα ἦσαν υἱοὶ τοῦ Ἰακώδ, ἐξ ὥνπερ αἱ δώδεκα φυλαὶ γεγένηνται, ΡΟ ρ. სარწმუნოვებითა მარჯულ ქრისტესა პოვნილ. ხოლო პირველი იგი ერი. რომელნი შჯულითა იქადოდეს. მარცხენით დადგინებულ რომლისა თვს ვერ გულის-გმა-ეყო იოსებსა ყოფადიე იგი წინაისწარმეტყუელისა მის გან. ჰგო–
existence, he divided the two sons of Joseph into two tribes; and the tribes were from then on thirteen in number, the tribe of Joseph having been divided between his two sons. And thus it was not without having something to do (here), Paul, the Apostle (115). Chosen (116) from the number of the tribes (Benjamin) he was counted thirteenth after the Apostles, and thus he was, to the Gentiles, as an Apostle, sent. Saying this, the prophet has openly shown us a very great mystery. For in the fact of having crossed his hands and (thus) made the younger of Joseph's sons, I mean Ephraim, pass to his right, but Manasseh, the elder, to his left, he has, through them, shown that there existed two calls and two peoples;
ნებდა ვიოარმედ დაავიწყდა მამასა მას. რომლისა თ»ს უპყრა გელი მისი, და ჰრქუა. არა ეგრე მამაო. არამედ ესე არს პირმშოი. დასდევ მარჯუენე გელი შენი თავსა ზედა მისსა და მან ჰრქუა. უწყი შვილო. უწყი. ესეცა დიდ იყოს და ამაღლდეს. ხოლო ძმაი მისი უმრწემესი უმაღლეს მისა იყოს, და დასუა Τ ἃ ეფრემი ზეშთა მანასესა არამედ რამეთუ გამოუჩინებელი დიდ არს წიგნთა შინა წმიდათა და უშურველად გამოვთქუათ რომლითა სიტყ»თა δὸ- კოჯთარ იყვნა ორნი იგი ძენი იოსებისნი რამეთუ ჰრქუა მას. ორნი ეგე შვილ– ნი. რომელ გამოგდეს შენ გან ეგჯპტეს. კიდრე შთასლვადმდე ჩემდა ეგჯპტედ. ჩემდა იყვნენ ეფრემ და მანასე. რუბენის და სჯმეონის–ებრ იყვნედ ჩემდა და სხუა ამიერითგან. რავდენ გესხნენ. შვილნი შენდა იყვნენ ამას მოგუას– ἀπὸ μημ სენი, κε ყღთთნეს 6ით.ითი+ ს დიე μιρ δὶς ეჩითოის ვინი, ფასადის, ἀπηπάπι μηῖι, თი με μεϊμειῖν თყსსბითყნი ნო ს შიMიემს Βηΐνιωι მაისს იცის βόα: ეხთL ნისსან ოგ ნი ნი ყნნთს ნ მიიღდნინშ ἱμωρδίμωρ᾽ ხმ პოთაყითL დიდს,
and that the younger, he, thanks to faith, is found at the right of Christ, while the older people, those who boast in the Law, are, by mutation, placed on the left. Whence Joseph, not having understood (108) in its spiritual significance what was being done there by the prophet, thinking that his father was mistaken (109), stopped him (110), and said: "Not so, father! For this one is the firstborn! Put your right (hand) on his head." But Jacob said: "I know, my child; he too will be great, he too will be exalted, but his brother, the younger, will be greater than him." And he placed (111) Ephraim before Manasseh. Also, since there are, in the Holy Scriptures, many things (112) in a hidden state, this too we will explain at length, namely what meaning the words have by which the blessed Jacob made the two sons of Joseph his own (113). The fact of saying, indeed: "the two sons born in Egypt before I came to you in Egypt, are mine; Ephraim and Manasseh, like Reuben and Simeon, shall be mine; as for the younger ones that you will beget (114) after them, they shall be yours" marks well that (that Jacob makes Ephraim and Manasseh his own). Since indeed Jacob had twelve sons, to whom precisely the twelve tribes owe their

Without any doubt when he says: "Gather yourselves together, that I may tell you what will happen to you in the last days" that is prophecy and not blessing.


[p. 52] {#po.27.p52}
იგი ყოფად არს თქუენდა მომავალთა ჟამთაო. ესე წინაისწარმეტყუელება არს δε χινυϊηῖι სილი" ჩ90ნ ძიეო ხეილი, ხL «იბხყნე 4ხყ ყხსჯ თ < თნჩივ τῶν ἡμερξν დგსცავიანაი ἐσεὶν τοῦτο, იბ» ΣΩΩΝΨ μὲν γὰρ εὐλογία >> φέρεται ἐπί τινι εὐλογουμένῳ, წ δὲ προφητεία γίνεται ἐπὶ ἔργῳ τινὶ τελουμένῳ. λεγούσης οὖν τῆς γραφῆς: « πάντες οὗτοι υἱοὶ Ἰακὼδ δώδεκα, καὶ >> ταῦτα ἐλάλησεν αὐτοῖς ὁ πατὴρ αὐτῶν καὶ εὐλόγησεν αὐτοὺς ἕκαστον, κατὰ τὴν εὐλογίαν αὐτοῦ ηὐλόγησεν αὐτούς », πῶς συμφωνήσει τὸ προχείμενον, ποτὲ μὲν προφητεῖαι δειχνύμεναι, ποτὲ δὲ εὐλογίαι ὀνομαζό- μεναι; ἐν τούτῳ δεῖ νοεῖν, ὅτι ἐν αὐτοῖς τοῖς ῥητοῖς καὶ εὐλογίαι κεῖνται καὶ προφητεῖαι, ἵνα αἱ μὲν εὐλογίαι καταντήσωσιν ἐπὶ τὸν ἐκ τοῦ Ἰούδα γενόμενον καὶ τὸν ἐν τῷ Ἰωσὴφ προτυπούμενον, τὸν ἐκ τοῦ Λευὶ ἱερέα τοῦ πατρὸς εὑρισκόμενον, αἱ δὲ προφητεῖαι ἐπὶ τοῖς τὰ ἐναντία πράξασιν καὶ τὸν υἱὸν τοῦ θεοῦ ἐξουδενήσασιν. და არა კურთხევა კურთხევაი, რაჟამს ვინმე იკურთხის. მის ზედა ითქ;ს.
For the blessing, it (118) concerns someone who is being blessed, while the prophecy is realized at the accomplishment of a certain fact. With these words of Scripture: "All these are the twelve sons of Jacob; <and> (119) this is how their father spoke to them and blessed them; he blessed them each according to his blessing (120), how will the (explanation) proposed above (121) agree: sometimes prophecies manifestly such, sometimes (prophecies) named blessings? (122) In this (explanation) it must be understood that it is in the very (things) expressed that lie both blessings and prophecies, in such a way that the blessings, they, fall upon Him who was born of Judah, and upon Him who is prefigured in Joseph, upon Him who, (descended) from Levi, is found to be Priest of the Father, while the prophecies (fall and remain) upon those who acted as adversaries and, for nothing, held the Son of God. Without any doubt when he says: "Gather yourselves together, that I may tell you what will happen to you in the last days" that is prophecy and not blessing.
ხოლო წინაისწარმეტყუელებაი იქმნის საქმისა თ»ს რაისმე აღსრულებისა და წიგნთა შინა უთქუამს. ვითარმედ ყოველნი ესე შენი იაკობისნი ათორ– მეტნი და ამას ეტყოდა მამაი იგი თ»სი და აკურთხნა იგინი თითოეულად მსგავსად კურთხევისა მათისა და ვითარმე ერთობა აქუნდეს ყოფადთა მათ ოდესმე წინაისწარმეტყუელებაი განცხადნეს. ანუ L ἃ ოდესმე კურთხევაი იგი ითქუას. ამით თანა-გუაც გულის-გკმის-ყოფაი ვითარმედ მათვე სიტყუათა შინა კურთხევანიცა არიან და წინაისწარმეტყუელებანიცა. რაითა კურთხევანი იგი დაადგრენ მის ზედა რომელი იუდაის გან იშვა და რომელი იგი იოსების მიერ მოგუესწავა და რომელი იგი ლევის გან მღდელ მამისა გამოვიდა. ხოლო წინაისწარმეტყუელებანი იგი მათ ზედა რომელნი იგი მგკდომად აღდგეს და ღმრთისაი შეურაცხ–ყვეს:.
For the blessing, it (118) concerns someone who is being blessed, while the prophecy is realized at the accomplishment of a certain fact. With these words of Scripture: "All these are the twelve sons of Jacob; <and> (119) this is how their father spoke to them and blessed them; he blessed them each according to his blessing (120), how will the (explanation) proposed above (121) agree: sometimes prophecies manifestly such, sometimes (prophecies) named blessings? (122) In this (explanation) it must be understood that it is in the very (things) expressed that lie both blessings and prophecies, in such a way that the blessings, they, fall upon Him who was born of Judah, and upon Him who is prefigured in Joseph, upon Him who, (descended) from Levi, is found to be Priest of the Father, while the prophecies (fall and remain) upon those who acted as adversaries and, for nothing, held the Son of God.

Reuben


[p. 53] {#po.27.p53}
ὌΒΕΝ Ὅτι δὲ οὕτως ἔχει αὐτὴ ἡ γραφὴ σαφέστερον διδάξει ἡμᾶς. φησὶν γάρ « Ῥουδὴμ πρωτότοκός μου σὺ ἰσχύς μου καὶ ἀρχὴ τέκνων μου, σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι καὶ σκληρὸς ოჯკუეთუ ესე ესრეთ არს. თ»თ წიგნნი გუასწაკებენ ჩუენ რამეთუ იტყუს.
Moreover, that it is indeed so, the Scripture itself will teach us more clearly (123). It says in fact: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you!"
რუბენ პირმშოი ჩემი შენ. დასაბამ და ძლიერება შვილთა ჩემთა. ფიცხლად ხყMს იო «ოყნა წ, ხსნის ხის «ხუნ იLსიLივნს ყბხი ცინ «თნ. /-ი.ანს –ეიი“ი“”იეეიიიეეეებ“ი- αὐθάδης" ἐξύδρισας ὡς ὕδωρ, μὴ ἐκζέσῃς: ἀνέδης γὰρ ἐπὶ τὴν κοίτην τοῦ πατρός σου, τότε ἐμίανας τὴν στρωμνήν, οὗ ἀνέθης ». τί οὖν, ἐροῦμεν προφητείαν τοῦτο ἢ εὐλογίαν; τὸ γὰρ λέγειν « σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι καὶ σκληρὸς αὐθάδης» καὶ τὸ « ἀνέδης ἐπὶ τὴν «( κοίτην > τοῦ πατρός σου" τότε ἐμίανας τὴν στρωμνήν, οὗ ἀνέθης » φαίνεται ἔλεγχος εἶναι μᾶλλον τοῦ γεγονότος πράγματος ἣ μέλλοντος γενέσθαι. ἀλλ᾽ ἐρεῖ τις, τοῦτο εἰρηκέναι ἐπειδὴ ἹῬουδὴμ. μετὰ Βάλλας τῆς παλλακῆς τοῦ πατρὸς αὐτοῦ κοιμηθεὶς ἐμίανεν τὴν κοίτην τοῦ πατρός. εὑρίσκεται οὖν κατὰ τὸν ἐκείνου λόγον ὁ προφήτης παρ « ῳχηχόσι πράγμασι, οὐ μέλλουσιν; ἐνθάδε παρ >> ακολουθῶν. ἔν γε τῷ εἰπεῖν αὐτόν᾽ « συνάχθητε υἱοὶ Ἰακώδ, ἵνα ἀπαγγείλω ὑμῖν τὰ συμδησόμενα ὑμῖν ἐπ᾽ ἐσχάτων τῶν ἡμερῶν », περὶ μελλόντων πραγ- μάτων τινῶν αἰνίττεται εἰπεῖν ὁ προφήτης. πῶς οὖν ἐνθάδε νομίσει τις αὐτὸν περὶ
My strength and the leader of my children! Hard in temper (124) and hard in arrogance! You have overflowed with force, like water. Do not boil (with passion)! For you went up to your father's couch; then you defiled the bed where you went up." What then? Shall we say that we have here prophecy? or blessing? For these words: "Hard in temper and hard in arrogance" and "you went up to your father's couch; then you defiled the bed where you went up" seem indeed to be blame for a thing having taken place, rather than (blame) for a thing about to take place (125). Hey! but! someone will say, (Jacob) said this once Reuben, after having slept with Bilhah, his father's concubine, had defiled his father's couch. By the account of this (opinion) the prophet is therefore <here> following by thought <past things, not future ones>. [What is certain] (126) is that, when he says: "Gather yourselves, sons of Jacob, that I may tell you what will happen to you in the last days" it is indeed about certain future things that the prophet lets it be understood that he is going to speak. How then, here, will someone think that he is speaking of a thing having taken place? As a prophet, in fact, it is on this account that he is judged a prophet,


[p. 54] {#po.27.p54}
πράγματος γεγονότος λέγειν; ὡς γὰρ προφήτης κατὰ τοῦτον τὸν λόγον κρίνεται προφήτης, აღიზარდე და ფიცხლად ამპარტავან. მაგინე ვითარცა წყალი. ნუმცა აღსდუღ– ნები: რამეთუ აჰგედ სარეცელსა მამისა შენისასა. მაშინ შეაგინე ცხედარი იგი რომელსა აჰტედ აწ რაიმე ვთქუათ. წინაისწარმეტყუელებამე არსა ესე ანუ კურთხევა და რომელ იგი თქუა. კითარმედ ფიცხლად მოიზარდე და ფიცხლად და ამპარტავანად და რომელ იგი თქუა. I ხ ვითარმედ აჰკედ სარეცელსა მამისა შენისასა. მაშინ შეაგინე ცხედარი იგი რომელსა ზედა აჰგედო. ოჯფროის რამეთუ შეტყუება ჩას ანუ ყოფილთა საქმეთა თვს. ანუ ყოფადთა თუს ვინ უწყის. თქუას ვინმე. ვითარმედ ამის თ»ს თქუაო. რამეთუ რუბენ დაწვა ბალაის თანა ზარჭისა მის მამისა თასისა და შეაგინა სარეცელი მამისა თუსისაი I უკუე იპოვების მსგავსად სიტყ»სა მის მისისა წინაისწარ– მეტყუვგლისა მის მიერ სხუაი სხუად თქუმულ და სიტყუასა მას მისსა. ვითარ– მედ შეკერბით მენი ეგე იაკობისნი. და გითხრა რაი იგი ყოფად არს თქუენდა შემდგომთა მათ ჟამთა. ამას ყოფადთა საქმეთა თ»უს რასამე იტყჟს წინაისწარ–- მეტყუელი იგი აწ ვითარმე შევჰრაცხოთ იგი ყოფილთა საქმეთა თჯ»ს წინა–
namely: when it is about future (things) that he makes his narrations, not about past things. One will not indeed regard as very strong (127) or extraordinary that, things, seen by us two or three years before, we recount them to others in detail, as if, to believe us, we were prophesying them, while with our own eyes we have seen the events. Let us therefore show the prophet <as a prophet> (128). He says in fact: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you! my strength and the leader of my children." And this, it is true, some (129) make it apply to the Savior, as being in reality the word of the Father addressing his own Son. But the things expressed immediately after will offend the ears of the readers, and if someone estimates that these (first) words aim at the Savior, <the words "hard in temper and hard in arrogance" and the following>, he will have to apply (them) (also to the Savior) (130). But it is not so at all. For Reuben was the firstborn son of Jacob, just as the people also were firstborn, [called] by the Law to <divine> adoption (131). The actions therefore of Reuben, the firstborn, (Jacob) has brought them closer to the (actions) which were to be those of the first people. For what he says: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you! my strength and the leader of my children" Jacob may have said it while addressing Reuben himself,

but "hard in temper and hard in arrogance" (he says it) because he penetrates by (prophetic) sight the spirit of indocility and insubordination (132) of his own race (Jacob's) (133). And, indeed, Moses, likewise said to them: "As for you, you are a stiff-necked people. Take heed to yourselves! Lest the Lord, angered (134), come to be angry and exterminate you." By saying: "You have overflowed with force, like water. Do not boil with passion" Jacob has beaten down (the pride) of the race which, according to the flesh, is theirs (135), for what they have (the people of this race) outraged the Law and treated with the final outrages the Word announced by it. For: "You went up," he says, "to your father's couch. Then you defiled the bed where you went up." Now "couch" and "bed," in his mouth, is the holy flesh of Christ, upon which the Saints, tasting, as on a holy divan, the rest, are saved (136); flesh which then these lawless ones, after having seized it, have outraged, offering to Him (to Christ) vinegar, and striking his head with a reed, and throwing on his back lashes of the whip, and spitting in his face (137), and flaying his

–_–_-–––>–_–_–....)..– ბინი» νομισθήσεται, εἰ τὰ πρὸ δύο ἢ τριῶν ἐτῶν ὑφ᾽ ἡμῶν ἑωραμένα, ταῦτα ὡς δῆθεν προφητεύοντες ἄλλοις ἐκδιηγούμεθα, ὑπὸ ὀφθαλμῶν ἑωρακότες τὰ γεγονότα. δείξωμεν οὖν τὸν προφήτην « ὡς προφήτην =>. λέγει γάρ᾽ « Ῥουδὴμ πρωτότοκός μου σὺ εἶ, ἰσχύς μου καὶ ἀρχὴ τέκνων μου ». καὶ τοῦτο μέν L τινες φέρουσιν ἐπὶ τὸν σωτῆρα, ὡς δῆθεν τοῦ πατρὸς προσομιλοῦντος τῷ ἰδίῳ υἱῷ. ἀλλὰ τὰ ἐπιφερόμενα ῥητὰ βλάψει τὰς ἀκοὰς τῶν ἐντυγχανόντων. « καὶ >> εἰ νομίσει τις τοῦτο πρὸς τὸν σωτῆρα εἰρῆσθαι, « « σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι καὶ σκληρὸς αὐθάδης » >> καὶ τὰ ἀκόλουθα ἐπισυνάψει᾽ ἀλλ᾽ οὐχ οὕτως ἔχει. ὁ
cheeks with slaps, and driving nails (138) into his hands. All that is what the impious and incredulous people did in concert with the princes of the priests and the scribes and all the leaders of the people. That is why the blessed prophet neither passed over in silence the work of these, nor wants to have anything in common with their wickedness and their counsel (138); but he withdraws for his part from their actions where enter such criminal machinations. <For he says thus:> (139) Moreover, that it is indeed so, the Scripture itself will teach us more clearly (123). It says in fact: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you!"
γὰρ ῬῬουδὴμ πρωτότοκος ἦν τοῦ Ἰακὼδ υἱός, ὡς καὶ λαὸς πρωτότοκος ἦν, διὰ τοῦ νόμου εἰς υἱοθεσίαν « θείαν >> κεκλημένος λαός. τὴν οὖν πρᾶξιν τοῦ ἹΡουδήμ, τοῦ πρωτοτόκου, συνέκρινεν πρὸς τὴν μέλλουσαν ἔσεσθαι τοῦ προτέρου λαοῦ. τὸ γὰρ εἰπεῖν αὐτόν" « ἹΡουδὴμ. πρωτότοχός μου σὺ εἶ, ἰσχύς μου καὶ ἀρχὴ τέκνων μου », δύναται πρὸς αὐτὸν τὸν Ρουδὴμ. ΡΟ ρ. მეტყუელებნ და არა ყოფილთა თ»უს. და არარაი დიდ არს და არცა დასაკ»რ– ვებელ. უკუეთუ წინაისწარ ორისა და სამისა წლისა ხილულ რაიმე არს ჩუენ შორის. სხუაი იგი წინაისწარმეტყუელებით სხუათა მიმართ მითხრობილ. და ჩუენ. თუალითა ვიხილნეთ ყოფილნი იგი აწ გამოვაჩინოთ წინაისწარმეტ– ყუელი იგი ვითარცა წინაისწარმეტყუელი რამეთუ იტყკს. რუბენ პირმშოი ჩემი შენ. შლიერებაი ჩემი. დასაბამი შვილთა ჩემთაი. და ამას რომელნიმე მაცხოვრისა ზედა წარმოიღებენ. ვითარმედ მამაი ზეგარდამო სიტყ»თა მით მესა თ»სსა ეტყუსო არამედ რომელ იგი შემდგომად სიტყუანი მოვლენან სავნებელ იქმნის ἃ მკითხველთა და მსმენელთა. და თუ ჰგონებდეს ვინმე მას ქრისტესა თქუმულად. რომელსა იგი იტყ»ს. ვითარმედ ფიცხლად
My strength and the leader of my children! Hard in temper (124) and hard in arrogance! You have overflowed with force, like water. Do not boil (with passion)! For you went up to your father's couch; then you defiled the bed where you went up." What then? Shall we say that we have here prophecy? or blessing? For these words: "Hard in temper and hard in arrogance" and "you went up to your father's couch; then you defiled the bed where you went up" seem indeed to be blame for a thing having taken place, rather than (blame) for a thing about to take place (125). Hey! but! someone will say, (Jacob) said this once Reuben, after having slept with Bilhah, his father's concubine, had defiled his father's couch. By the account of this (opinion) the prophet is therefore <here> following by thought <past things, not future ones>. [What is certain] (126) is that, when he says: "Gather yourselves, sons of Jacob, that I may tell you what will happen to you in the last days" it is indeed about certain future things that the prophet lets it be understood that he is going to speak. How then, here, will someone think that he is speaking of a thing having taken place? As a prophet, in fact, it is on this account that he is judged a prophet,
აღორმნდიო და ფიცხლად ამპარტავან და სხუასა მას განწესებულად ავნებდეს არამედ არა ეგრე რომელ ჰგონებენ ვითარმედ პირმშოი იყო იაკობისი. და ერი იგი პირმშო შჯულითა მით წოდებულ მკ»დრად ერად. და საქმენი იგი რუბენისნი პირმშოისა მის ამსგავსნა ყოფადთა მათ საქმეთა თ»ს პირველისა მის ერისა გან და რომელ იგი თქუა. რუბენ პირმშოი ჩემი შენ. მლიერებაი ჩემი და დასაბამი შვილებისა ჩემისაი შემმლებელ არს იაკობ რუბენის ზედა. ხსეხოყ. ხL ნ პხბ ხL იჯ ყტმსთ სჯ 6იმთიცხ, თითი ხს ყხლსიL ἔπε, ი გხყხი წი αὐταί ს პნსი ხლის" ფრის διωμημωμίριωϊω πα μπῇ ხობს, პხც თვ.6 თიხიL დხღის + სღ ყოხყვობ გ ახრეტატნს βρρίμ. ἡδωιριμωρί ε მიანის თინ. ჩ-იაცნს სასიოხისჩს ჩნ გი, ყრი: მს ხი ხL αἱ βαρῖν ოლეLიყ ჩმიყ + ყი იმს ხხ ს “გერ, ხ0ს ნივ საილინყრ ოლო ჩილიმ ინდი ἂψ იდ αἰ, იხდი: დის” ყმსნიყინ ცხყხთვ. ხროვის + σε. სმიივს πρὶ ყვი L «ხლ ხვს თხვნიიL, გIანით გნტნიიებ ხი გენით φωμωμεν, ἔκ. ყრყL ს რეს ყრდნივნ ε δᾳ გნ თიიყნი, გს რინ! αΔηρωῖβι იღეს წლ მისული ნ. ძი რაატეესს αἰωρνμῖμι, ἱιρβίωιρ ψμρηβηρηι მნის
know: when it is about future (things) that he makes his narrations, not about past things. Indeed, one will not regard it as very strong (127) or extraordinary that, regarding things seen by us two or three years before, we recount them to others in detail, as if, if we are to be believed, we were prophesying them, while with our own eyes we have seen the events. Let us therefore show the prophet «as a prophet» (128). He says in fact: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you! My strength and the leader of my children." And this, it is true, some (129) apply to the Savior, as being in reality the word of the Father addressing his own Son. But the things expressed immediately after will offend the ears of the readers, and if someone believes that these (first) words aim at the Savior, <the words "hard in temper and hard in arrogance" and the following ones, he would have to apply (them also to the Savior) (130). But it is not so at all. For Reuben was the firstborn son of Jacob, just as the people were also firstborn, the one called by the Law to (divine) adoption (131). The actions, therefore, of Reuben, the firstborn, (Jacob) has compared to the (actions) which were to be those of the first people. For what he says: "Reuben, my firstborn, it is you! My strength and the leader of my children," Jacob may have said it while addressing Reuben himself,

cheeks with slaps, and driving nails (138) into his hands. All this is what the impious and incredulous people did in concert with the chief priests and the scribes and all the leaders of the people. ὁ Ἰακὼθ εἰρηκέναι. τὸ δὲ « σκληρὸς φέρεσθαι, καὶ σκληρὸς αὐθάδης » ἐνορῶν τὸ πνεῦμα τὸ ἀπειθὲς καὶ ἀνυπότακτον τοῦ γένους αὑτοῦ. καὶ γὰρ Μωῦσῆς ὁμοίως πρὸς αὐτοὺς ἔφη" « ὑμεῖς δὲ λαὸς σκληροτράχηλός ἐστε. προσέχετε αὑτοῖς, μήποτε θυμωθεὶς ὀργισθῇ κύριος <= καὶ -> ἐξολοθρεύσῃ ὑμᾶς ». ἐν δὲ τῷ εἰπεῖν τὸν Ἰακώδ’ « ἐξύθρισας ὡς ὕδωρ, μὴ ἐκζέσῃς » ἐμείωσεν αὐτῶν τὸ κατὰ σάρκα γένος, διὰ τὸ ἐνυδρίσαι αὐτοὺς τῷ νόμῳ καὶ ἐξυθρίσαι αὐτοὺς τὸν διὰ αὐτοῦ κηρυχθέντα λόγον. « ἀνέθης γὰρ » λέγει « ἐπὶ κοίτην τοῦ πατρός σου, τότε ἐμίανας τὴν στρωμνήν, οὗ ἀνέθης». κοίτην δὲ καὶ στρωμνὴν εἴρηκεν τὴν ἁγίαν σάρκα Χριστοῦ, ἐφ᾽ ἣν οἱ ἅγιοι ὡς ἐπὶ κλίνην ἁγίαν ἀναπαυόμενοι σώζονται: ἣν τότε λαθόντες « οἱ >> ἄνομοι ἐνύδρισαν ὄξος τούτῳ προσφέροντες καὶ καλάμῳ κεφαλὴν τύπτοντες καὶ μάστιγας νώτῳ ἐπιδάλλοντες καὶ εἰς πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ ἐμπτύοντες καὶ ῥαπίσμασιν σιαγόνας δέροντες καὶ ἥλους χερσὶν ἐμπηγνύοντες" ταῦτα πάντα ἔδρασεν ὁ ἀσεδὴς καὶ ἄπιστος λαὸς σὺν ἀρχιερεῦσιν καὶ γραμματεῦσιν καὶ ΡΟ ρ. ხოლო რომელი Simeon Levi
Ξ ΙΕ. « Συμεὼν καὶ Λευεὶ ἀδελφοί, συνετέλεσαν ἀδικίαν ἐξαιρέσεως αὐτῶν. εἰς βουλὴν αὐτῶν μὴ ἔλθοι ἣ ψυχή μου « καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπισυστάσει αὐτῶν μὴ ἐρίσαι τὰ ἥπατά μου" >> ὅτι ἐν τῷ θυμῷ αὐτῶν ἀπέκτειναν ἀνθρώπους καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπιθυμίᾳ ἐνευροκόπησαν ταῦρον. ἐπικατάρατος ὁ θυμὸς αὐτῶν, ὅτι αὐθάδης, καὶ ἡ μῆνις αὐτῶν, ὅτι ἐσκληρύνθη. διαμεριῶ αὐτοὺς ἐν Ἰακὼδ καὶ διασπερῶ αὐτοὺς ἐν Ἰσραήλ. » სყმეონ და ლევი შმათა აღასრულეს უშჯულოვებაი შეთქუმულებითა თ»სითა. ზრახვასა მათსა ნუ შეერთვინ სული ჩემი და კრებასა მათსა ნუ ეზიარებინ შინაგანი ჩემი. რამეთუ რისხვითა თ»სითა მოწყდეს კაცი და გულის-წყრო- მითა თჯ»უსითა კირნი დაჰკუეთნეს კუროსა. იწყევენ გულის-წყრომაი მათი. რამეთუ ამპარტავანებით იყო და განრისხვბაი მათი. რამეთუ სიფიცხლით იყო განვყვნე იგინი შორის იაკობსა და განვაბნინე შორის ისრაზლსა:. სხმნი! κα მ.;Lს ნიყოიიტც, ἐψανεπειιρένα βῖν κωΐμειιι ρέεῖιτι θεῖν διριιιριιῖιτι შნირი μι ρἐκιωφῖι. ს μηρφπιρήω სიყრ ბს მიენ თს“ ხშ, κε ს ძიცი/ სივი მს 6იიასიონხენს სხტი;სც სა, 4L ს დიდოიემხიის μι ρίμωδᾳ იის διωρηβίμ, ხL ს ყია ოი βέμιαι ხიდნიისყ მი სოთიდ იიტჩს ყვის სსსბნი μιωρίμει θεῖν სიყრ ს φαμωραν, ხL ყთიმს სიყ ყს Mხიი ნ აძისნეტყ ვსიხ ს მიც, L ვლიLხყხყ ფით ,იტინე_ « Συμεὼν καὶ Λευεὶ ἀδελφοί, συνετέλεσαν ἀδικίαν ἐξαιρέσεως αὐτῶν. εἰς βουλὴν αὐτῶν μὴ ἔλθοι ἣ ψυχή μου « καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπισυστάσει αὐτῶν μὴ ἐρίσαι τὰ ἥπατά μου" >> ὅτι ἐν τῷ θυμῷ αὐτῶν ἀπέκτειναν ἀνθρώπους καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπιθυμίᾳ ἐνευροκόπησαν ταῦρον. ἐπικατάρατος ὁ θυμὸς αὐτῶν, ὅτι αὐθάδης, καὶ ἡ μῆνις αὐτῶν, ὅτι ἐσκληρύνθη. διαμεριῶ αὐτοὺς ἐν Ἰακὼδ καὶ διασπερῶ αὐτοὺς ἐν Ἰσραήλ. » სყმეონ და ლევი შმათა აღასრულეს უშჯულოვებაი შეთქუმულებითა თ»სითა.
"Simeon and Levi are brothers. They have committed together an injustice by killing as they did (140). Into their counsel let not my soul go! <And into their assembly let not my heart quarrel!> (141). Because in their wrath they killed men (142), and, in their covetousness (143), hamstrung the bull. Cursed be their wrath, because it is arrogant; and their fury, because it has hardened! I will divide them in Jacob, and disperse them in Israel." "Simeon and Levi are brothers. They have committed together an injustice by killing as they did (140). Into their counsel let not my soul go! <And into their assembly let not my heart quarrel!> (141). Because in their wrath they killed men (142), and, in their covetousness (143), hamstrung the bull. Cursed be their wrath, because it is arrogant; and their fury, because it has hardened! I will divide them in Jacob, and disperse them in Israel."

But it is not so. And, indeed, they themselves, then, for their defense, represented to their father that they had done there only a just work. For, to the words of their father: "You have made me hateful, to the point of making me a wicked man in the eyes of all the inhabitants of the land, and towards the Canaanites (147) and towards the Perizzites. Now, I am numerically the weakest; and, once leagued against me, they will cut me to pieces and I will be smashed to bits, I and my people (148)," they replied with these words: "Then? As a prostitute, will they abuse our sister?" It is because they are worthy of praise rather than blame, by reasoning thus, they who, in the full age of youth, have thus, boiling with anger, taken up the cause for their sister then deflowered by the son of Shechem, that Jacob also, and from his mouth, well after these (events), by graciously making a gift of the city

to the blessed Joseph, said (149): "As a choice portion, in preference to all your brothers, I give you Shechem, which I took from the hand of the Amorites with my sword and my bow." So much so that (150) Jacob also, himself, attests that the suppression of the Shechemites is justice.

ხსბისე სიყრ, ხ ილრხყის MილნიLლ ჯი ს I ხლთ ხ.ლხისყ. თხ. ციტნივრიგ ღილს ἣν ნ ὅπη ε
Read the Gospel and you will find it written. From the tribe of Simeon were the Scribes; and from that of Levi, the chief priests. Since, therefore, it is by the counsel of these and the resolution they took there, that Christ was delivered and by them put to death, knowing this in advance, the prophet says: "Into their counsel let not my soul go!" Now he speaks there of the counsel where they deliberated, seeking from where they could launch an accusation against Jesus, so that after having seized him (155) by ruse, they could kill him. As also Isaiah says (it): "Woe to their soul! because in their counsel they have taken against themselves a bad resolution, saying: "Let us put the just one in bonds, since with us he shows himself intractable."
ს Ἰου-ΊΥΡ. « Καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐπισυστάσει αὐτῶν μὴ ἐρίσαι τὰ ἥπατά μου »" τότε γὰρ ἐπι- συνήχθησαν ἐπ᾽ αὐτὸν πάντες οἱ ἀρχιερεῖς καὶ οἱ γραμματεῖς καὶ οἱ πρεσδύτεροι τοῦ λαοῦ ἐπὶ τὴν οἰκίαν τοῦ ἡγεμόνος, αἰτούμενοι θάνατον κατ᾽ αὐτοῦ. ΡΟ ρ.
"And into their assembly let not my heart quarrel (156)." Then, indeed, against him, all (157) the chief priests and the scribes and the elders of the people gathered at the governor's house, demanding the death penalty against him.

"And, in their covetousness, hamstrung the bull." Now, by saying "their covetousness," he means "the covetousness (they have had) of the world," they who, for having coveted earthly goods, have lost the heavenly ones, once they had killed the Prince of Life, like a bull. ἡ. ᾿Ἐπεὶ οὖν φθάσαντες εἴπομεν, ὅτι ἐν αὐτοῖς τοῖς ῥητοῖς αἱ μὲν εὐλογίαι κεῖνται, αἱ δὲ προφητεῖαι, εὔλογόν ἐστιν πεῖσαι τοὺς φιλομαθεῖς, μὴ λόγῳ μόνον ἐπιδεῖξαι, ἀλλὰ δυνάμει ῥητῶν φανερῶσαι τὰ προκείμενα. λέγει γὰρ εὐλογῶν τὸν Ἰούδαν οὕτως. « Ἰούδα, σὲ αἰνέσαισαν οἱ ἀδελφοί σου" αἱ χεῖρές σου ἐπὶ νώτου τῶν ἐχθρῶν σου’ προσκυνή- σουσίν σοι οἱ υἱοὶ τοῦ πατρός σου. καὶ σκύμνος λέοντος Ἰούδα, ἐκ βλαστοῦ, υἱέ μου, ἀνέ- βης. ἀναπεσὼν ἐκοιμήθης ὡς λέων καὶ ὡς σκύμνος" τίς ἐγερεῖ αὐτόν; οὐκ ἐκλείψει ἄρχων ἐξ Ἰούδα, οὐδὲ ἡγούμενος ἐκ τῶν μηρῶν αὐτοῦ, ἕως ἂν ἔλθῃ ᾧ ἀπόκειται, καὶ αὐτὸς ἔσται προσδοκία ἐθνῶν' δεσμεύων πρὸς ἄμπελον τὴν საათია αὑτοῦ καὶ τῇ ἕλυκι τὸν πῶλον და რამეთუ წინაისწარ ვთქუთ. ვითარმედ მუნვე თქუმულთა მათ შინა არიან კურთხევანი იგი. ოჯმჯობეს არს დამორჩილებად სწავლის მოყუარეთა, არა თუ სიტყჯთ ხოლო გამოჩინებით. არამედ შალით-ურთ თქუმულთა მათ განცხა– დებულად რაი იგი წინა-გჯც რამეთუ ეტყყს კურთხევით იუდას ესრე. იუდა. შენ გაქებენ შმანი შენნი. კელნი შენნი ბეჭთა ზედა მტერთა შენთასა. თაყუა–- ნის–გცემდენ შენ ძენი მამისა შენისანი ლეკუ ლომის იუდა აღმოცენებისა გან. შვილო ჩემო. გამოჰგედ. აჰკედ. ინაკ-იდგ ვითარცა ლომმან და ლეკუმან ლომისამან ვინ აღადგინოს იგი. ნუ მოაკლდებინ მთავარი იუდაის გან და არცა გელმწიფტ წყვილთა მისთა გან. ვიდრემდის მოვიდეს რომელსა იგი ემარ–
Since, therefore, in advance (162), we have said that it is in the terms themselves (which express them) that, on the one hand, lie blessings, on the other hand, prophecies (163), it is reasonable, to well persuade those who have the desire to be instructed, not only to demonstrate by reason, but, by the very force of the terms, to make evident, these propositions (164). For (Jacob), in blessing Judah, speaks thus: "Judah, you, may your brothers praise you! Your hands (are) on the back of your enemies. Your father's sons will adore you." Then (165): "Lion's whelp (is) Judah. From a shoot, my son, you have gone up. After having bent your back, you have lain down (166), like a lion and (167) like a lion's whelp. Who will awaken him? There will not lack a leader issued from Judah, nor a conductor (of the people) gone out from his thigh; until that one comes to whom it is reserved (to be); and he, will be (168) the expectation of the nations. There he is, attaching to the vine his donkey and to the orb (of the vine-branch), the foal of his donkey. He will wash in wine his robe, and in the

There will not lack a leader issued from Judah, nor a conductor (of the people) gone out from his thigh; until that one comes to whom it is reserved (to be); and he, will be (168) the expectation of the nations. ხთ გხლიმისყე სიდი, მსსჯნL სსწხყL πρπι ὃ ნ. ხL სხ ნ ჯოგი იიყვიაყ. თიხიყნ ფიუმიჯ ნ; βὲμ δὲ. ცვენ ყრი სის. იCხყნ βῆναι πρὶ ფხყთიმი ბის ხ.ი ხC ციხის τῆς ὄνου αὐτοῦ. πλυνεῖ ἐν οἴνῳ τὴν στολὴν αὐτοῦ καὶ ἐν αἵματι σταφυλῆς τὴν περιβολὴν αὐτοῦ" χαροποιοὶ οἱ ὀφθαλμοὶ αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ οἴνου" καὶ λευκοὶ οἱ ὀδόντες αὐτοῦ γάλα ».
blood of a ripe grape his garment. With joy, the wine makes his eyes shine; and white are his teeth more than milk (169)."


[p. 70] {#po.27.p70}
სქარრსაკია ρει εν. და სისხლითა ყურმნისაითა სამოსელი თკსი მხიარულ არიან თუალნი მისნი ვითარცა დკნითა და სპეტაკ კბილნი მისნი უფროის სმისა:. ΡΟ ρ. ϑιμηπηπι, ყე სრსნენ μὲ ρ. გიCიუმთვნს ხს «ჯი ჩხ ს ἡβῖνεπι, δὲ თხო თთბი.სი “ἭΝ ––_წეიეი Ἐπεὶ οὖν φθάσαντες εἴπομεν, ὅτι ἐν αὐτοῖς τοῖς ῥητοῖς αἱ μὲν εὐλογίαι κεῖνται, αἱ δὲ προφητεῖαι, εὔλογόν ἐστιν πεῖσαι τοὺς φιλομαθεῖς, μὴ λόγῳ μόνον ἐπιδεῖξαι, ἀλλὰ δυνάμει ῥητῶν φανερῶσαι τὰ προκείμενα. λέγει γὰρ εὐλογῶν τὸν ᾿Ιούδαν 1Ζ. οὕτως. « Ἰούδα, σὲ αἰνέσαισαν οἱ ἀδελφοί σου" αἱ χεῖρές σου ἐπὶ νώτου τῶν ἐχθρῶν σου" προσχυνή- σουσίν σοι οἱ υἱοὶ τοῦ πατρός σου. καὶ σκύμνος λέοντος Ἰούδα, ἐκ βλαστοῦ, υἱέ μου, ἀνέ- βης. ἀναπεσὼν ἐκοιμήθης ὡς λέων καὶ ὡς σκύμνος" τίς ἐγερεῖ αὐτόν; οὐκ ἐκλείψει ἄρχων ἐξ Ἰούδα, οὐδὲ ἡγούμενος ἐκ τῶν μηρῶν αὐτοῦ, ἕως ἂν ἔλθῃ ᾧ ἀπόκειται, καὶ αὐτὸς ἔσται προσδοκία ἐθνῶν' δεσμεύων πρὸς ἄμπελον τὴν თაბ ბაი αὑτοῦ καὶ ყე ἕλυκι τὸν πῶλον და რამეთუ წინაისწარ ვთქუთ. ვითარმედ მუნვე თქუმულთა მათ შინა არიან კურთხევანი იგი. ოჯმჯობეს არს დამორჩილებად სწავლის მოყუარეთა. არა თუ სიტყჯთ ხოლო გამოჩინებით. არამედ შალით-ურთ თქუმულთა მათ განცხა– დებულად რაი იგი წინა-გჯც რამეთუ δ კურთხევით იუდას ესრე. იუდა. შენ გაქებენ შმანი შენნი. კელნი შენნი ბეჭთა ზედა მტერთა შენთასა. თაყუა–- ნის–გცემდენ შენ შენი მამისა შენისანი ლეკუ ლომის იუდა აღმოცენებისა გან. შვილო ჩემო. გამოშჰკედ. აშტედ. ინაკ-იდგ ვითარცა ლომმან და ლეკუმან ლომისამან ვინ აღადგინოს იგი. ნუ მოაკლდებინ მთავარი იუდაის გან და არცა გელმწიფტ წყვილთა მისთა გან. ვიდრემდის მოვიდეს რომელსა იგი ემარ– ხვის და იგი.იყოს სასო ხ ნათესავთა. გამოაბას ვენაგსა კარაული თ»სი და მასკნესა ვენატისასა კიც» კარაულისაი განრცხეს ღ»ნითა სამკაული თ»სი ნ. How is it possible that the things expressed here are of the same nature as the things said above against Reuben? There, in fact, he says: "Hard <in temper, and hard> (170) in arrogance, you have overflowed with strength, like water. Do not bubble (with passion). For you went up to your father's couch; then, you defiled the bed where you went up." Then (against Simeon and Levi) what is said: "Cursed be their wrath, because it is arrogant; and their fury, because it has hardened!" These are not, in fact, words of blessing, but indeed a reproach, a prophecy being there, regarding them (Reuben, Simeon, Levi) revealed (171). To say on the contrary: "Judah, you, may your brothers praise you! Your hands (are) on the back of your enemies" and (172): "Your father's sons will adore you," that, yes (173), seems indeed to be a blessing.
